Same-type and structural-branch counts are lower bounds. Roughly one in five or six sampled rejections was judged related; these small samples do not estimate a population error rate. In step 3 search every family member and the accepted item-level assessed/component union links (R5-03), not only recorded relations. A rejection means relation not established; a templated rejection reason is not source comparison evidence.

Textbook questions remain internal. Syllabus statements bound scope; examination parts and award rows establish observed demands and official marking; textbook methods and planner implications are separate author judgements.

## Six-function properties, domains and reciprocal Pythagorean choices

Demands: 8227:2020:M2:T3:O01:D01, 8227:2020:M2:T3:O01:D02, 8227:2020:M2:T3:O02:D01.

### Teaching across the books

Goldie starts reciprocal definitions, exact values and Pythagorean identities, then equations and sign/existence explanations. YM revisits definitions inC4and beginsC10with reciprocal sketches and suitable-pair uniqueness arguments. Those visual arguments require both curves and a reason, not merely a calculated root.

Demands without an NSSCAS part: none.

### Difficulty and grade descriptors

E12/C12/A13name all six functions and reciprocal identities. Start with definitions/exact values; harder sources introduce branch domains, a shared-graph uniqueness proof or identity choice. Avoid adding formal function theory beyond the syllabus.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

cot iscos/sinand has zeros at90/270degrees; representing it as1/tan everywhere creates false holes. Goldie3C11/YMTry16Q2 keys miss90degrees after dividing bycos. Original csc/sec poles must still be excluded.

### Gaps and representations

Goldie has explicit sign-possibility discussion; YM has more reciprocal sketch scaffolding. A graph comparison is distinct from a numeric-equation drill. Visuals need coherent poles, branch extrema and angular units.

## Compound identities in exact evaluation, proofs and solving

Demands: 8227:2020:M2:T3:O02:D02, 8227:2020:M2:T3:O02:D05.

### Teaching across the books

Goldie gives a geometric sine proof and asks which assumptions it uses, then derives other formulas and applies them in exact values, equations and geometry. YM gives triangle teaching derivations, forward/reverse patterns and signed two-angle tasks. Both need conditional tangent denominators, not just the memorised numerator pattern.

Demands without an NSSCAS part: none.

### Difficulty and grade descriptors

E12/C12/A13permit compound identities for simplification, exact evaluation and solving. Harder sources change signs, require a derived ratio first, or construct the equation from geometry; exact special values alone are the entry branch.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

YM8TryQ2 swaps keys and omits one answer; its other TryQ1 printed working has a wrong sign even though the final values agree. Goldie3B3(i) has a mixed-variable stem;3B9 swaps ordered roles. Keep each as a source defect rather than an accepted learner path.

### Gaps and representations

Goldie has more assumption/coordinate-geometry work; YM gives explicit signed quadrant scaffolding. Forced complex-method facets inC11remain extra-scope original evidence even if an ordinary trig proof reaches the same result.

## Double-angle choices and derived higher/half forms

Demands: 8227:2020:M2:T3:O02:D03.

### Teaching across the books

Goldie develops all three cosine-double forms, explicitly distinguishes checking from proving and motivates square reduction for calculus. YM includes repeated doubles, half-angle signs, triple proofs and connected proof/hence equations. Derived forms are legitimate consequences rather than added memorisation objectives.

### Examination comparison

Directly assessed NSSCAS parts: 8227:EXAM:2021:P2:Q12:b, 8227:EXAM:2022:P2:Q10:b, 8227:EXAM:2022:P2:Q7:a, 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q3:b.i, 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q7:a.

(easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:double-angle-bounded-equations to 8227:EXAM:2021:P2:Q12:b — Both rewrite a double-angle tangent/reciprocal expression into a polynomial in tan(x), retain original poles and return bounded angles. Demands without an NSSCAS part: none.

### Difficulty and grade descriptors

E12/C12/A13bound double-angle use. Harder source branches select a productive form, derive3x/4x or control a half-angle sign. General unbounded solutions remain outsideLIMIT02.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

Dividing bycosx can lose a valid original cotangent zero; introducingtan2x can create an unnecessary proof gap. The original angle interval decides the sign of a half-angle root; YM13TryQ4 prints an invalid plus branch.

### Gaps and representations

Goldie explicitly links square reduction to later integration without asking an integral there; YM gives more exact half/double ratio practice. A teaching formula derivation is not a separately examined integration task.

## R-form, bounded solving and transformed extrema

Demands: 8227:2020:M2:T3:O02:D04.

### Teaching across the books

Goldie emphasises requested phase templates, arbitrary-quadrant phases and whole conversion/extrema/sketch/solve chains. YM separates degree/radian forms, multiple arguments and nonlinear range mapping. Both teach acute R triangles only under sign conditions; a general coefficient claim needs quadrant-aware phases and a nonzero pair.

### Difficulty and grade descriptors

E12/C12/A13name R-form use. Entry tasks compare coefficients; harder chains add phase windows, first-positive arguments, graphs or nonmonotone range maps. The current taxonomy is not a grade classification or approved branch count.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

Goldie3D6 phase67.7is wrong; YMTry19Q2 omits a root; Try20Q1 changes a sign between parts. A square minimum can be at innerzero, not the negative endpoint. Complete current cancellation has no unique phase.

### Gaps and representations

Goldie corridor geometry and current superposition offer modelling variety; YM offers many explicit nonlinear extrema. Repeated source occurrences with new tariffs do not create additional types. A diagram needs the same width/projection values as the algebra.

## Completed form, vertex and quadratic sketch

Demands: 8227:2020:M1:T1:O01:D01, 8227:2020:M1:T1:O01:D02, 8227:2020:M1:T1:O01:D03.

### Teaching across the books

YM separates prescribed completion, identifying the extreme value and its abscissa, and drawing a consistent sketch. Example22 changes the outer coefficient;Example23 changes the orientation. It then reverses the direction in graph-to-equation examples. Goldie2&3 largely assumes this Mathematics1background:Exercise1B23 asks the greatest value of a quadratic obtained by subtracting two cubics, but does not supply a parallel standalone quadratic-sketch sequence. GoldieC6Prompt1 merely permits algebraic solution methods; choosing completion in an author solution is not evidence that the source assesses completion.

### Difficulty and grade descriptors

GradeE01 explicitly includes completion, turning point and sketch;GradeC01 emphasises interpretation of completed form. YM develops from monic forms to scaled/fractional vertices and linked sketches. Goldie1B23 is harder through preceding coefficient recovery, not through larger numbers. GradeA01’s coupled algebra provides contextual upper-demand evidence, not a numeric parameter domain or a grade assigned to each textbook item.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

YM Try21Q1 has a correct coordinate with a wrong guide label; this is a source defect, not learner-error prevalence. Proposed feedback should distinguish wrong outer scaling, confusing the minimum ordinate with the attaining x, and a graph contradicting otherwise correct coordinates. No examiner-specific frequency is claimed for those three distinctions.

### Gaps and representations

YM has explicit sketch practice;the inspected Goldie census has no directly assessed standalone quadratic-sketch item. The exam completion/vertex chain is therefore supported mainly by YM. A graph supplied for reconstruction is different evidence from a graph produced as an answer.

## Discriminants, parameter conditions and universal root proofs

Demands: 8227:2020:M1:T1:O02:D01, 8227:2020:M1:T1:O02:D02, 8227:2020:M1:T1:O02:D03.

### Teaching across the books

YM gives sign/root-count tables before fixed-equation classification, parameter ranges, and universal proofs. Example15 has one parameter condition;Try16Q3 makes the leading coefficient vanish at a candidate parameter. Example19’s real-parameter rationality claim is false;Try19 supplies integer restrictions that change what can be proved. Goldie uses quadratic factors inside polynomial work but has no corresponding directly assessed discriminant/parameter sequence in the inspected inventory; that incidental method must not be promoted to an extra direct demand.

Held/defective evidence, not usable learner anchors: 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q1:b.

### Difficulty and grade descriptors

GradeE01 covers computing a discriminant, whileC01 adds interpretation of real roots. A01 supports more coupled algebra and coefficient-domain reasoning, but does not state a numerical ceiling for parameter coefficients. YM’s hard boundary is logical:linear degeneration, a zero discriminant, or rational versus merely real coefficients. Do not classify difficulty by coefficient magnitude alone.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

A square is nonnegative, not always positive. YM’s source commentary and several proofs omit zero or linear cases; these are registered source shortcomings. ERR01 corroborates the need for complete proof and a conclusion, but does not establish how often each textbook-specific mistake occurs among learners.

### Gaps and representations

YM provides both routine fixed-equation drills and stronger universal proofs. A given-root coefficient-recovery drill is prerequisite algebra, not automatically the discriminant parameter-condition demand. Held NSSCAS2023P1Q1(b) is not a valid exemplar; its hold remains binding even though the syllabus demand has other sources.

## Identity meaning and coefficients in a specified form

Demands: 8227:2020:M1:T1:O03:D01, 8227:2020:M1:T1:O03:D02.

### Teaching across the books

YM explicitly contrasts identities, conditional equations and inequalities in its teaching tables, then asks coefficients in prescribed square, product and rational forms. GoldieC7Prompt1 explicitly asks why an identity symbol changes to equality when a particular value is substituted; that retained prompt is within8227even though the surrounding unrestricted partial-fraction method is not. Goldie8E4/8H3 supply the rational decomposition template before coefficient determination and later integration. Given template and free choice of a decomposition are different scope decisions.

### Examination comparison

Directly assessed NSSCAS parts: 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q3:answer, 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q2:answer.

Same-type textbook practice: 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:polynomial-specified-identity to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q2:answer.

Structural branches: 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:rational-specified-identity to 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q3:answer — The source clears one irreducible quadratic and one linear denominator and recovers three constants through a quadratic polynomial identity. The exam has two linear denominators and two constants, yielding a linear coefficient system or direct substitution at the roots. The source adds a higher numerator degree and a third coupled unknown. (harder).

Demands without an NSSCAS part: 8227:2020:M1:T1:O03:D01.

### Difficulty and grade descriptors

GradeC01 names the distinction and coefficient determination;A01 adds two or more coupled unknowns. Routine prescribed-square constants are easier than simultaneous product coefficients, correlated sign pairs or a template followed by an integral. The level descriptor does not justify requiring general partial fractions or complex roots.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

An identity must hold throughout its common domain; rational denominator exclusions remain after clearing denominators. A conditional equality is not a different mathematical species from every identity:the distinction is all admitted inputs versus a condition to solve. YM’s explanatory table needs that qualification. These are source/author reasoning notes; no examiner-specific identity misconception is asserted without a matching report excerpt.

### Gaps and representations

The explicit identity-distinction task comes from an otherwise excluded chapter, showing why scope must be checked at facet level. Conversely, many coefficient exercises do not ask the conceptual distinction and must not erase that gap. Textbook integration chains extend the observed coefficient part rather than establish a new exam award pattern.

## Quadratic transformations and original-variable recovery

Demands: 8227:2020:M1:T1:O04:D01, 8227:2020:M1:T1:O04:D02, 8227:2020:M1:T1:O04:D03.

### Teaching across the books

YM progresses from equations quadratic inx² to nonnegative square-root substitutions, reciprocal powers, positive exponentials and a nested rational expression. It separately teaches original radical equations where squaring creates candidates needing rejection. Goldie1B8 factors a quartic as a quadratic inx², but the source asks factorisation and zero count; the inventory does not claim that every intermediate recognition step is an independently assessed substitution demand.

### Examination comparison

Directly assessed NSSCAS parts: 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q3:a, 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q3:b, 8227:EXAM:2021:P2:Q11:b, 8227:EXAM:2022:P1:Q2:b, 8227:EXAM:2022:P2:Q2:answer, 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q1:answer, 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q11:b.

Same-type textbook practice: 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:exponential-positive-substitution to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q1:answer; 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:exponential-positive-substitution to 8227:EXAM:2022:P2:Q2:answer.

Structural branches: 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:quadratic-transformation-recovery to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q3:a — The source quartic has a nonzero x-squared term and requires solving a genuine quadratic in x-squared; the exam binomial x⁴-81 can be solved by direct fourth-root/difference-of-squares reasoning. The source adds the intermediate quadratic decision. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:quadratic-transformation-recovery to 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q11:b — The source Try Now12(a) is exactly the same quartic x⁴-13x²+36. It asks only for its real roots; the exam additionally selects the positive roots as the labelled intersection abscissae a and b. The source lacks that final graphical selection. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:radical-equation-domain-filter to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q3:b — The source isolates a square root, squares, solves the resulting quadratic and rejects an extraneous original-equation root. The exam instead inherits fourth-power roots, equates sqrt(a)-4 to each and checks root signs. The source adds initial root elimination and extraneous-candidate testing, rather than using the hence scaffold. Demands without an NSSCAS part: none.

### Difficulty and grade descriptors

GradeA01 explicitly names recognising and solving an equation quadratic in a function ofx. Ordinary prior quadratic solving is a scaffold, not a substitute for that demand. The harder textbook cases change the intermediate range or inversion multiplicity:even powers, odd real roots, reciprocal exclusion and a positive exponential are genuine structural differences. No grade-specific numerical parameter bounds are decided here.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

Negative square-root/exponential intermediate values must be rejected; odd-root negatives may be valid; reciprocals exclude zero. YM Example10’s extraneous squared candidate andTry13’s different inverse ranges provide concrete contrast. ERR10 supports the recovery issue broadly, not an inferred diagnosis from any one wrong final answer. Goldie1B8’s wrong first factorisation is a source defect; a later correct count does not validate the erroneous step.

### Gaps and representations

The retained Goldie book provides factorisation context but not the full YM progression of directly assessed transformation/recovery tasks. Branch breadth therefore comes mainly from YM plus the examined HENCE chain. A reciprocal equation reduced by denominator clearing must keep its original forbidden values.

## Recover quadratic and cubic equations from sufficient information

Demands: 8227:2020:M1:T1:O05:D01, 8227:2020:M1:T1:O05:D02.

### Teaching across the books

YM separates root-factor reconstruction from vertex-led reconstruction, then introduces cubics with crossing/touching roots and an extra scaling point. Multiple curves share axes inExample27, requiring careful label/scale reading. Goldie1B7 reverses its usual supplied-polynomial sequence:a given cubic graph fixes multiplicities and scale before a new equation is solved. Goldie11B9(i) retains a real-root quadratic construction, while its later complex-root cases remain excluded.

### Examination comparison

Directly assessed NSSCAS parts: 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q2:answer.

Same-type textbook practice: 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:quadratic-reconstruction-roots to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q2:answer; 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:quadratic-reconstruction-vertex to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q2:answer.

Structural branches: 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:polynomial-equation-from-roots to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q2:answer — The source expands prescribed symbolic root factors to deduce a monic quadratic. The exam must read roots -2,4 from the diagram and determine a nonmonic scale from vertex (1,-3); the extra graph reading and scale recovery are not present in the source. (easier).

Demands without an NSSCAS part: 8227:2020:M1:T1:O05:D02.

### Difficulty and grade descriptors

GradeC01 says recover an equation from sufficient graph information;A01 explicitly mentions quadratic and cubic graphs. YM’s simple roots/intercept cases are less connected than rational touching factors, three simultaneous curves or mixed constraints requiring a coefficient system. GradeE01’s quadratic completion/sketch statement does not itself warrant cubic-reconstruction difficulty. These are qualitative source comparisons, not assigned item grades.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

A touching root carries multiplicity; its factor scaling must be compensated globally. The source’s categorical advice against a fractional linear factor is misleading:equivalent scaled factors are valid when the outer coefficient changes consistently. A scaling point that is already a zero does not determine the scale. These are mathematical/author cautions; no specific examiner-error frequency is claimed.

### Gaps and representations

Both books supply cubic reconstruction although the local examined sample does not. A drawing supplied as data and a drawing required as a response are distinct. Graph-derived root data, a prose vertex and purely algebraic root information should retain their original representation labels.

## Critical values, intervals and boundary evidence

Demands: 8227:2020:M1:T1:O06:D01, 8227:2020:M1:T1:O06:D02.

### Teaching across the books

YM develops an inequality from a quadratic graph or factorisation, explicitly contrasts upward/downward signs and often requests a number line. ItsTry29Q4 first supplies an equation-solving scaffold with2dp endpoints. Goldie’s retained algebra chapter concentrates on modulus inequalities rather than a parallel standalone quadratic-inequality sequence; their squared comparisons can use quadratic sign analysis as a component, but that does not automatically count as direct quadratic-interval coverage.

Demands without an NSSCAS part: none.

### Difficulty and grade descriptors

GradeC01 explicitly includes solving quadratic inequalities. YM’s harder cases add collection across both sides, negative outer coefficients, rounded displayed roots and connected equality-first structure. A01/A11provide related compound algebra and modulus contexts, but do not assign a grade solely from two intervals rather than one. E01 supplies supporting quadratic skills without expressly naming the whole interval demand.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

Endpoint inclusion follows the original strict/nonstrict relation. YM Try29Q1(b)’s filled dots conflict with its strict textual key;Try29Q3has a wrong supplementary boundary label. These are source defects. A generic wrong-root diagnosis would miss a correct boundary with an incorrect open/closed endpoint; no examiner-specific prevalence is claimed.

### Gaps and representations

The inspected inventory has direct quadratic-inequality practice inYM, whileGoldie’s modulus questions supply related components. Exam comparisons should preserve the difference. The line/curve parameter branch additionally needs a geometric interpretation and can include linear degeneration.

## Basic and transformed trig graphs

Demands: 8227:2020:M1:T6:O01:D01, 8227:2020:M1:T6:O02:D01, 8227:2020:M1:T6:O02:D02, 8227:2020:M1:T6:O02:D03.

### Teaching across the books

YM uses base-function plotting investigations, separates a/b/c effects, then moves from features to sketches and supplied-graph deductions. GoldieC3 begins at reciprocal graphs/R-form applications and assumes these foundations. The source investigations retain all rows rather than counting each parameter row as a new type.

### Examination comparison

The exam draws one vertically translated and scaled double-frequency sine curve. Source outputs include extra intersection algebra, whereas the exam requires amplitude/vertical-shift interpretation absent from the source. Demands without an NSSCAS part: none.

### Difficulty and grade descriptors

E06 supplies basic trig graphs;C06/A06 include transformed amplitude/period/sketch/interpretation. Harder source chains add named coordinates, inequalities, a suitable chosen line or multiple functions. Keep those branches within the actual small-integer/simple-fraction scope.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

Tangent has no amplitude; the visible window need not contain a full cycle or a global extremum. Try15Q1 and Exercise4.2Q5 have wrong printed curve labels. Simultaneous sign changes can leave a recovered formula unchanged unless signs are restricted.

### Gaps and representations

YM has unusually broad technology/table practice; Goldie offers complementary reciprocal/R-form visual work. These investigations support explanations but are not all observed exam structures. Required sketch responses stay distinct from worked-solution pictures.

## Exact special angles and connected geometry

Demands: 8227:2020:M1:T6:O03:D01.

### Teaching across the books

YM derives45 and30/60 values from exact triangles, then uses related angles, ratio arithmetic and connected geometry. Goldie3A begins with reciprocal special values and3B compounds them into75/15degree values. A derived non-special compound angle is not the same reasoning as a memorised basic value.

### Examination comparison

Directly assessed NSSCAS parts: 8227:EXAM:2021:P2:Q6:b, 8227:EXAM:2022:P1:Q9:b, 8227:EXAM:2022:P2:Q3:a, 8227:EXAM:2022:P2:Q3:b, 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q3:a, 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q3:b.ii, 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q12:b, 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q2:answer, 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q9:b, 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q3:answer.

A checked same-type exact-value counterpart is 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:reciprocal-exact-values against 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q3:b.ii: Goldie chapter 3 Exercise 3A Q2(i) and Q2(iii), PDF page 74 / printed page 60. The selected responses evaluate cotangent and cosecant at related special angles exactly; the exam asks for cosec(2pi/3)+cot(2pi/3). Reciprocal definitions, quadrant signs and exact surd arithmetic are the shared decisions. Adding the two exact values is ordinary arithmetic variation. The original exam target is a numerical exact value, not the integral in part b(iii).

The other directly linked parts embed special values in different complete tasks: bounded trigonometric equations, compound-angle work, or geometry. Their exact-value DD link demonstrates that special values are assessed; it does not make a basic value drill the same type as the complete connected task. The selected-response ledger records 4 same-type and 45 structural-branch family/part comparisons against the active parts listed above. These counts describe recorded comparisons, not distinct recipes, examination frequency or an accepted coverage score.

Demands without an NSSCAS part in this section: none. The previous absence-of-match proposal is withdrawn: a concrete counterpart has now been read and recorded.

### Difficulty and grade descriptors

C06 explicitly names exact special/related angles. The easiest branch recalls/evaluates a ratio; harder diagrams require choosing the correct triangle and connecting an inverse angle relation. That does not add general unsupported geometry.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

Try18Q1(c) key answersCD rather thanBC. Try28 ratios need positive radius and signed coordinates. A requested squared ratio does not require the same sign selection as an unsquared one.

### Gaps and representations

YM has more basic diagram scaffolding; Goldie adds reciprocal/compound variety. Keep each right-angle, midpoint and collinearity fact explicit and consistent with the generated diagram.

### Proposals for step 3

Use the checked Goldie 3A Q2 / 2023 P2 Q3(b)(ii) selected responses as an exact reciprocal-evaluation comparison when planning. Preserve exact rather than decimal answers, radians/degrees, defined reciprocal values and quadrant signs. Textbook items remain internal evidence; later recipe references require branch-level review.

Keep a graduated progression visible: basic 30/45/60 values; related-angle signs; reciprocal values and exact combinations; then compound-angle, bounded-root and diagram-connected tasks. Decide whether these are separate recipes or embedded modules in step 3. A non-special angle obtained by a compound identity and a basic special value are different decisions, even when both final answers are surds.

Do not replace the observed full tasks with isolated arithmetic drills. Compare the actual selected textbook responses with each examination part, and use the explicit extra decisions to set scaffolding and difficulty. Retain the accepted source-item union for overlapping assessed/component DD tags until planning resolves the registry overlap; do not retag items merely to improve match counts.

These are evidence-backed suggestions for planning, not an approved recipe split, marking contract, generated diagram or learner-display permission.

### Teaching across the books

YM states principal ranges, gives exact/calculator illustrations, then solves unknown-input inverse equations and restricted inverse-function/graph chains. GoldieC11Activity8 supplies a retained principal-arctangent/range exercise amid otherwise excluded complex-number material.

E06 explicitly includes principal inverse notation. The hard source branches add polynomial input and branch-restricted inverse graphs while retaining the same finite principal ranges.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

Try53 asks degrees but prints radians. Source principal-range reminders have label/endpoint defects. atan never attains plus/minuspi/2. Squaring an input equation can add invalid candidates.

### Gaps and representations

YM offers coherent progression; Goldie gives a targeted retained facet. Inverse graph sketches need original restriction and reflected endpoints, not just a symbolic inverse formula.

## Basic-identity proof structures

Demands: 8227:2020:M1:T6:O05:D01.

### Teaching across the books

YM explicitly distinguishes expansion/grouping, fractional aggregation, conjugates and both-side/common-expression methods. Goldie starts reciprocal Pythagorean identities and builds compound/double identities. A numerical check is useful for an error, not a proof of an identity.

### Examination comparison

Directly assessed NSSCAS parts: 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q5:b, 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q8:a, 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q6:a.

Same-type textbook practice: 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:identity-expansion-basic to 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q6:a; 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:identity-fraction-cancellation to 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q8:a; 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:identity-fraction-cancellation to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q5:b; 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trig-proof-hence-solve to 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q6:a.

Structural branches: 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:identity-conjugate-square to 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q8:a — The source first combines reciprocal terms and squares the resulting binomial before cancelling a difference-of-squares factor. The exam can directly rewrite tangent and factor sin²; reciprocal combination and squaring are additional source steps. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:identity-common-expression to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q5:b — The source independently normalises its rational left side and fourth-power right side to one common expression. The exam can transform its left side directly to the given product; the second independent normalisation/fourth-power factorisation adds source work. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trig-proof-hence-solve to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q5:b — The source proves (tan(x)+1)/(sin(x)tan(x)+cos(x))=sin(x)+cos(x), then reuses it to solve a bounded equation. The exam asks only for its own general identity proof. The rational simplification route and identity target differ; the source also assesses an additional bounded-angle response. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trig-proof-hence-solve to 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q8:a — The source proves (tan(x)+1)/(sin(x)tan(x)+cos(x))=sin(x)+cos(x), then reuses it to solve a bounded equation. The exam asks only for its own general identity proof. The rational simplification route and identity target differ; the source also assesses an additional bounded-angle response. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trig-proof-hence-solve to 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q6:a — Source Exercise4.2 Q9(a) is exactly the exam cubic identity; the connected source also requires a hence equation in (b). Only the explicit (a) exception carries same-type proof equivalence. (harder).

Demands without an NSSCAS part: none.

### Difficulty and grade descriptors

C06/A06 name proving with basic identities. The hard end changes the proof route and domain control, not merely the number of terms. Do not assume Bloom from the word prove.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

Cancellation may narrow or remove original domain points; a simpler right-hand side cannot extend an original quotient identity. Squared or conjugate routes need nonzero conditions and must not assume the target.

### Gaps and representations

YM provides extensive proof-route practice; Goldie stresses checking versus proving. Similar-looking final expressions can need different evidence and should remain method branches.

## Transform equations and enumerate complete bounded solutions

Demands: 8227:2020:M1:T6:O05:D02, 8227:2020:M1:T6:O06:D01, 8227:2020:M1:T6:O06:D02.

### Teaching across the books

YM progresses from one ratio through squared values, negative windows, scaled/shifted arguments, zero factors, quadratics and proof/hence chains. Goldie3B/3C adds compound/double-angle reductions; the full interval and original domain still decide which roots survive.

(harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trig-intersections-analytic to 8227:EXAM:2022:P2:Q3:a — Both select a pictured trigonometric root after a one-ratio quadratic. The source must first equate tan(x) and cos(x), reduce to the sine quadratic and calculate both named intersections including B's ordinate. The exam supplies its sine-quadratic curve and seeks only the particular x-axis crossing A. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trig-coefficients-zeros-sketch to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q5:a — The source first recovers both coefficients of a+bcos(2x), isolates its cosine zeros with doubled-argument interval recovery, then sketches the curve. The isolated exam solve returns roots of a quadratic in one untransformed ratio, with no coefficient-recovery or sketch output. Each version has a different algebraic reduction, and the source adds a graph response. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trig-coefficients-zeros-sketch to 8227:EXAM:2022:P1:Q9:b — The source first recovers both coefficients of a+bcos(2x), isolates its cosine zeros with doubled-argument interval recovery, then sketches the curve. The isolated exam solve returns roots of a quadratic in one untransformed ratio, with no coefficient-recovery or sketch output. Each version has a different algebraic reduction, and the source adds a graph response. The isolated exam solve returns roots of a quadratic in one untransformed ratio, with no coefficient-recovery or sketch output. Each version has a different algebraic reduction, and the source adds a graph response. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trig-coefficients-zeros-sketch to 8227:EXAM:2022:P2:Q3:a — The source constructs a cosine curve from supplied exact function values, calculates its two zeros and draws it. The exam supplies the sine-quadratic curve and asks for one particular intercept. The source adds coefficient recovery, a changed-argument root pair and a sketch; the exam needs quadratic ratio filtering. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trig-quadratic-unspecified-interval to 8227:EXAM:2022:P1:Q9:a — Both demonstrate a Pythagorean quadratic rewrite, but the source starts with cosine versus sine-squared. The exam additionally rewrites tan(x) and clears the original cosine denominator; the source lacks that reciprocal/denominator step. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trig-quadratic-unspecified-interval to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q12:a — The source starts with 3cos(x)=2sin²(x) and shows 2cos²(x)+3cos(x)-2=0. The exam reaches that identical equation only after replacing cot(x) and multiplying by the nonzero sine. The source rewrite omits that initial reciprocal-domain preparation. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:polynomial-root-discovery to 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q5:c — The source stops after finding an integer polynomial root; the exam must complete factorisation and recover all bounded trig angles from a transformed polynomial. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:polynomial-factorisation to 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q5:c — The factorisation stage is shared. The exam continues to a reciprocal-trig substitution, rejects impossible ratios and enumerates the strict doubled-angle interval; the source stops at polynomial factors. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:polynomial-factor-solve to 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q5:c — The polynomial factoring/root stage is shared. The exam then solves f(cosec 2theta)+6=0 on a strict degree interval: reject impossible transformed ratios, handle reciprocal signs and enumerate every doubled-angle branch. The source returns only the cubic x-roots. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:polynomial-composed-recovery to 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q5:c — The book factors the supplied polynomial and solves its single-angle composition. The exam must first recognise the offset polynomial f+6 from the preceding remainder and then solve a cosecant equation in a doubled angle over the stated range. Those decisions are absent from the book selection. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:polynomial-supplied-factor-test to 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q5:c — The exam infers and completes a factorisation of f+6, then filters and solves a cosecant-double-angle equation on a strict interval. The source only establishes one supplied factor. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:polynomial-bounded-integer-root to 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q5:c — The source finds one promised real integer root on a bounded interval. The exam additionally factors its entire adjusted polynomial and transforms every admissible ratio into bounded trig angles. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:polynomial-supplied-factor-completion to 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q5:c — The source completes an ordinary polynomial factorisation. The exam must additionally recover every admissible angle from its cosecant-double-angle polynomial equation. (easier).

Demands without an NSSCAS part: none.

### Difficulty and grade descriptors

C06/A06 include small-integer/simple-fraction multiples and equations easily reducible to basic forms. Increase difficulty by changing the admissible branches, argument window or required reduction, not by inventing an unbounded general-solution requirement.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

Cancellingcos/sin loses valid zeros; taking one square-root sign loses ratio branches. Conversely clearing original poles can add false roots. Repeated polynomial roots and inclusive endpoints must be deduplicated correctly.

### Gaps and representations

YM gives more foundation/windows; Goldie enriches identity-choice and contextual compound forms. Graph counting and analytic root listing remain different response demands; no sampled exam absence removes a syllabus-required demand.

## Geometric radian meaning and conversion

Demands: 8227:2020:M1:T5:O01:D01, 8227:2020:M1:T5:O01:D02.

### Teaching across the books

YM PDF154 explicitly shows an arc equal to its radius and six unit-radian arcs plus a remainder. Its unnumbered conversion then uses a complete turn. Goldie retained advanced-trigonometry pages assume this unit; no corresponding retained basic-radian exercise was found. YM teaching figures provide explanation, not a posed definition exercise.

### Examination comparison

Directly assessed NSSCAS parts: none claimed.

Same-type textbook practice: relation not established for the recorded exemplars.

Structural branches: relation not established for the recorded exemplars.

Demands without an NSSCAS part: 8227:2020:M1:T5:O01:D01, 8227:2020:M1:T5:O01:D02.

### Difficulty and grade descriptors

Start with arc/radius meaning before conversion; harder decimals test precision, not a higher cognitive level. Scope is the ordinary geometric unit, not arbitrary analytic angle conventions. E05 explicitly bounds radian interpretation/conversion; C05/A05 bound arc, sector and segment applications. These statements set limits, not a grade label for every source occurrence.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

Confusing radians with a length or entering degrees into s=r theta changes the answer. These are concept-based author feedback leads; this statement does not assert an examiner-reported frequency.

### Gaps and representations

The definition has teaching support in YM but no separately posed definition response in the retained population. This is an honest practice gap even though the source census contains the relevant diagram.

## Arc length, sector area and geometric inverse problems

Demands: 8227:2020:M1:T5:O02:D01, 8227:2020:M1:T5:O02:D02.

### Teaching across the books

YM proceeds through direct minor/major arcs, major-sector area, perimeter/area inverse problems, then overlapping circles and composite plates. Goldie advanced chapters use geometry in applications but do not replace this explicit circular-measure progression. Worked diagrams and source-given diagrams retain distinct roles.

### Examination comparison

Directly assessed NSSCAS parts: 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q7:a, 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q7:b, 8227:EXAM:2022:P1:Q8:a, 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q6:b, 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q7:b, 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q10:b.

Same-type textbook practice: relation not established for the recorded exemplars.

Structural branches: 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:circle-arc-major-minor to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q7:a — The source computes minor and complementary major arc lengths for one fixed radius. The exam must select arcs at two different radii, exclude the internal radial lengths and add only the two exposed straight pieces before simplifying a symbolic perimeter. The source lacks the composite boundary selection. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:circle-major-sector to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q7:b — The source finds one complementary major-sector area from a supplied minor angle. The exam additionally computes and adds a minor sector at twice the radius; the source omits this second piece. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:circle-major-sector to 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q6:b — The source complements a minor angle and finds one major-sector area. The exam adds a separate smaller-radius minor sector, using a radius already derived by tangent geometry; the source omits that second area. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:sector-perimeter-inverse to 8227:EXAM:2022:P1:Q8:a — The source removes two radii from the perimeter, obtains the missing angle, then uses it for area at a fixed numerical radius. The exam keeps radius variable, obtains theta=(60-2r)/r and substitutes to prove A=30r-r^2. The source is the easier numerical precursor without symbolic elimination/generalisation. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:annular-sector-model to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q7:a — The source constructs an outer radius from radial width, infers the common angle and traces two same-angle arcs plus two width edges. The exam instead joins a minor outer sector to a complementary major inner sector, so its inner arc uses 2pi-alpha rather than alpha. The source adds angle inference; the exam adds complementary-major boundary selection. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:annular-sector-model to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q7:b — The source obtains an unknown angle and subtracts concentric sectors with the same angle, also returning a perimeter. The exam adds a complementary major inner sector to an outer minor sector with values given. The source adds reconstruction/perimeter; the exam changes the region assembly to complementary-sector addition. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:annular-sector-model to 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q6:b — The source reconstructs radii/angle and subtracts same-angle concentric sectors. The exam uses a known outer radius and inherited tangent radius, then adds a major outer sector and a minor inner sector. The source adds reconstruction/perimeter outputs; the exam requires complementary-angle addition, not annulus subtraction. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:overlapping-sector-area to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q7:b — The source uses two sectors with distinct centres and subtracts an enclosing trapezium to isolate their overlap: sector+sector-trapezium. The exam uses the complementary assembly trapezium-sector-sector to isolate a gap between tangent circles. The source must obtain both sector areas/angles in the connected chain; the exam inherits its first angle. Region assembly and scaffolding differ. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:curvilinear-perimeter to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q7:a — The source plate combines a right triangle and one sector; its full chain derives a radius, traces the external perimeter and computes area numerically. The exam plate has two complementary-angle sectors and asks only for a symbolic perimeter. The source adds trigonometric reconstruction/area, while the exam adds two-radius arc selection and symbolic simplification. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:curvilinear-perimeter to 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q10:b — Both identify the labelled circular regions, express their areas using correct radii/angles and form an area ratio. The book must first obtain the two complementary curvilinear areas from arcs with different centres; the exam uses the radius relation already obtained in a and compares a complete inner circle with one sector, cancelling r^2. These additional region-subtraction decisions make the book branch harder. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:segment-and-chord to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q7:b — Only source a(iii), the sector-area response, is compared. It evaluates one sector from a given angle/radius; the exam adds an outer minor sector and complementary inner major sector. This is easier supporting sector arithmetic and does not cover the plate assembly. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:segment-and-chord to 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q6:b — Only source a(iii), the sector-area response, is compared. The exam must choose complementary major/minor angles and add sectors at different radii. The source evaluates just one already specified sector and omits the composite-region decision. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:triangle-minus-one-sector to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q7:b — The source recovers an acute angle in its right triangle and subtracts one contained sector from the triangle. The exam subtracts two unequal tangent-circle sectors from a trapezium using its inherited angle. The source has one sector and simpler containing polygon; it is an easier region-decomposition precursor. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:single-circle-sector-plus-triangles to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q7:b — The source uses the angles about one centre to recover a residual sector and adds two equal central triangles. The exam adds major/minor sectors at different radii and has no triangle-area contribution. The source adds central-angle inference and triangle trigonometry to its numerical area assembly. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:single-circle-sector-plus-triangles to 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q6:b — The source derives a residual central angle and combines a sector with two triangles. The isolated exam area part uses the preceding radius and combines only two sectors with complementary angles. The source adds angle reconstruction and triangle-area terms. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:three-tangent-sector-gap to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q7:b — The source identifies a 3-4-5 triangle from three externally tangent circles, obtains all three corner angles and subtracts three sectors from it. The exam has two circles over a common tangent, uses a trapezium and subtracts two sectors after inheriting an angle. The source adds a third radius/sector and unscaffolded three-angle recovery. (harder).

Demands without an NSSCAS part: none.

### Difficulty and grade descriptors

The accessible end uses one formula with a stated radius/angle; harder sources require reconstructing a radius, converting an inverse-trig angle and selecting several regions. Grade descriptors bound later parameters; present groupings do not certify grade levels. E05 explicitly bounds radian interpretation/conversion; C05/A05 bound arc, sector and segment applications. These statements set limits, not a grade label for every source occurrence.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

Major/minor substitution, internal seam perimeter and mixing two arc centres are source-specific hazards. Rounding a derived length before using it in the area can spoil the final required precision.

### Gaps and representations

YM provides the principal explicit practice. Goldie absence here is not a syllabus omission. The tangent wedge, annular sector and two-centre overlaps change the geometry and must not be flattened into number substitutions.

### Teaching across the books

YM first derives exact sector/triangle/chord quantities in Example8, then combines a major segment and new semicircle in Try8Q2, and poses a symbolic equal-area relation in Example9. Goldie retained scope does not offer an equivalent basic segment sequence.

### Examination comparison

Directly assessed NSSCAS parts: none claimed.

Same-type textbook practice: relation not established for the recorded exemplars.

Structural branches: relation not established for the recorded exemplars.

Direct segment subtraction is the entry branch. Symbolic area equality, major-segment selection and the new semicircle radius introduce additional decisions; none requires expanding beyond syllabus circular geometry. E05 explicitly bounds radian interpretation/conversion; C05/A05 bound arc, sector and segment applications. These statements set limits, not a grade label for every source occurrence.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

Try8Q1 names an undefined B where the diagram uses Y. Keep the correction as a source defect, not a wrong learner inference. A semicircle built on a chord generally has a different radius from the original circle.

YM contains useful original-diagram templates and symbolic variety; source originals remain internal. A fixed image cannot be paired with unrelated generated radii/angles.

## Elementary standard derivatives, nesting and real domains

Demands: 8227:2020:M2:T4:O01:D01, 8227:2020:M2:T4:O01:D02.

### Teaching across the books

Goldie motivates naturalderivatives throughln/expinverse relationships and trigderivatives throughgradientgraphs,then gives standard/composite drills. YM first introduces algebraic product/quotientrules,then e,lnandtrigfunctions,explicitly separating sums,products and quotients. Both show alternative loglaw routes,but a positive-only expansion cannotreplace a chain route on a larger originaldomain. Standard radiansine/cos/tan are prescribed;reciprocaltrig derivative proofs use those premises rather than invent mandatoryrecall.

### Examination comparison

Directly assessed NSSCAS parts: 8227:EXAM:2021:P2:Q5:a, 8227:EXAM:2022:P2:Q3:b, 8227:EXAM:2022:P2:Q5:a, 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q5:a, 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q9:a, 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q10:a, 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q4:a, 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q7:c, 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q10:a, 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q11:c, 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q5:answer.

Same-type textbook practice: 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:elementary-composite-derivative to 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q4:a; 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:implicit-given-point-gradient to 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q5:answer; 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:parametric-fixed-parameter-gradient to 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q7:c; 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:numerical-gradient-constraint-chain to 8227:EXAM:2022:P2:Q5:a.

Structural branches: 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:product-polynomial-radical-derivative to 8227:EXAM:2021:P2:Q5:a — The source applies the product rule to two algebraic factors and simplifies. The exam applies it to x ln(x), also differentiates the subtracted x and recognises cancellation. The source omits the logarithmic derivative and the additive cancellation. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:product-polynomial-radical-derivative to 8227:EXAM:2022:P2:Q5:a — The source supplies explicit polynomial factors and asks only for their product derivative. The exam product includes an exponential and must then impose a gradient and rearrange it logarithmically. This is easier product-rule support, not the complete fixed-point derivation. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:quotient-algebraic-derivative to 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q5:a — The source differentiates an explicit algebraic quotient and returns its derivative. The exam logarithmic quotient additionally requires the ln derivative, solving derivative zero with domain checks, and recovering the stationary ordinate. The source supports quotient ordering only. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:elementary-standard-sum-derivative to 8227:EXAM:2022:P2:Q3:b — The source differentiates 3sin(x) directly. The exam sine-square curve additionally requires a power-chain contribution and exact evaluation at the selected crossing A. The source covers only the elementary sine term, not the whole gradient response. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:elementary-standard-sum-derivative to 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q9:a — The source asks directly for the derivative of e^x. The exam exponential has a nonunit negative inner coefficient and the learner must interpret velocity-to-acceleration. The source omits both the inner factor and the physical rate interpretation. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:elementary-standard-sum-derivative to 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q10:a — The source differentiates 2ln(x). The exam also has ln(3x+10), requiring an affine chain factor, then solves the combined rational derivative for an admissible stationary abscissa. The simple rule drill does not cover that chain. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:elementary-standard-sum-derivative to 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q11:c — The source asks for the derivative of e^x. The decay exam adds a negative scaled time argument, evaluation at t=3, context units and the positive magnitude of a decrease. None of those extra decisions is assessed in the source single-rule response. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:elementary-composite-derivative to 8227:EXAM:2022:P2:Q3:b — Only source Try Now 18 Q2(a) is compared: differentiate a power of sin(x) with its inner derivative. The exam adds another sine term and evaluates exactly at the inherited graph crossing. The source power-chain response is easier support without the exact-point calculation. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:elementary-composite-derivative to 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q9:a — The source differentiates a scaled decreasing exponential. The exam asks for acceleration from its exponential velocity; that physical derivative choice and units are additional to the explicitly cued source differentiation. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:elementary-composite-derivative to 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q10:a — The source differentiates ln(x^2+1) by its inner chain rule. The exam combines two logarithmic derivatives and then solves an original-domain stationary equation. Its rational simplification and stationary root response are additional. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:elementary-composite-derivative to 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q10:a — Only Try Now 18 Q2(a) is compared: use a power/trigonometric chain derivative. The exam uses tan rather than sin and additionally rewrites sec-squared through an identity to prove a specified cubic-tangent form. The source has no target-form proof or identity choice. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:elementary-composite-derivative to 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q11:c — The source differentiates a scaled decreasing affine exponential symbolically. The exam additionally evaluates at a given time, interprets the signed derivative as a positive decrease magnitude and supplies the rate units. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:elementary-product-derivative to 8227:EXAM:2021:P2:Q5:a — Source Example 16(a) differentiates x ln(ln(x)), so its product term includes a nested logarithmic chain. The exam differentiates x ln(x)-x, where the product contribution cancels the subtracted derivative. The source adds a nested log and the exam adds cancellation; neither full response is identical. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:elementary-product-derivative to 8227:EXAM:2022:P2:Q5:a — The source differentiates 5x exp(-2x), retaining both product terms and the affine exponential factor. The exam differentiates 6x exp(x/3), then sets a prescribed gradient and rearranges to the supplied logarithmic recurrence. The source lacks the condition/rearrangement responses. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:elementary-quotient-derivative to 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q5:a — The source obtains the derivative of ln(x)/x. The exam instead has x/ln(x), so the reciprocal quotient order changes, then it must solve derivative zero on x>0, x not 1 and recover exact coordinates. Source quotient-rule differentiation is support only. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trapezium-stationary-preparation to 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q5:a — Source (a) finds the abscissa from an unquotiented x-2ln(x). The exam differentiates 5x/ln(x), must collect the quotient-rule numerator and return both stationary coordinates. The source omits the quotient and ordinate decisions. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trapezium-stationary-preparation to 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q10:a — Source (a) and exam both differentiate logarithmic expressions and solve the zero derivative for an abscissa. The source gives 1-2/x=0; the exam has two reciprocal-linear derivative terms, one with the affine-inner chain coefficient, and must combine their denominators. The source is the simpler branch. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:explicit-prescribed-gradient-points to 8227:EXAM:2022:P2:Q5:a — The source differentiates a polynomial product, sets its derivative to -5 and solves for its abscissae. The target uses 6x*exp(x/3), imposes slope20 and proves a fixed-point logarithmic rearrangement instead of solving algebraically. Polynomial derivative inversion is easier supporting gradient-condition practice. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:explicit-second-derivative-zero to 8227:EXAM:2021:P2:Q5:a — The exam asks only for the first derivative of xlnx-x, using product rule then cancellation. The source differentiates an explicit product twice, sets its second derivative to zero and returns original coordinates; those additional response decisions make this a harder branch. The exp versus log family alone is not the distinction. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:explicit-second-derivative-zero to 8227:EXAM:2022:P2:Q5:a — Both differentiate an explicit x-times-exponential curve and impose a derivative condition. The source differentiates twice, solves zero second derivative and returns original coordinates. The exam differentiates once, sets the gradient to20 and must rearrange to the prescribed p=3ln(10/(p+3)) form. Source derivative order/coordinate recovery and exam logarithmic rearrangement make a mixed branch; no arbitrary difference-count rejection is used. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:implicit-dependent-expression to 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q5:answer — Goldie differentiates separate expressions xy, 3x²y³ and sin y with y dependent on x, retaining dy/dx in each output. 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q5:answer differentiates a full relation and collects/isolates dy/dx; it then substitutes the given point for a numerical gradient. The elementary implicit-expression practice is a scaffold, not coverage of the complete curve response. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:implicit-curve-derivative to 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q5:answer — The source Example28 derives an implicit exponential-product expression but stops at the general derivative. The target uses one exponential-times-y product plus y^2 and additionally evaluates its gradient at a supplied point. The source has a second product and a y^3 term; the target adds numerical point evaluation. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:implicit-point-gradient-line to 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q5:answer — The source GoldieExample16 derives the implicit gradient of y^3+xy=2, evaluates it at a fully given point and constructs a tangent. The target differentiate 2exp(3x)*y+y^2=15 implicitly and evaluate its gradient at the supplied point (0,-5). The source adds its tangent/derivative-expression response, while the target uses an exponential product but asks for gradient only. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:implicit-linked-normal-curve-sketch to 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q5:answer — Both differentiate an implicit product and isolate dy/dx. Q6(i) has the simpler affine product (x-6)(y+4)=2 and returns a general expression. The exam product 2e^(3x) y also needs exponential chain differentiation, includes y², and requires evaluation at (0,-5). Thus the named derivative subpart is an easier branch. The gradient calculation embedded in ii supports transfer, but the asked normal line is not silently treated as a gradient-only response; ii–iv are excluded. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:parametric-regular-derivative to 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q7:c — The source Goldie4E Q1(i) returns the general quotient (dy/dt)/(dx/dt) for polynomial parameter coordinates, without a point/line condition. The target additionally differentiate the supplied exponential parameter coordinates and use the previously iterated parameter to return a gradient to 2sf. This is easier supporting quotient construction and does not cover that target selection/evaluation response. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:parametric-general-tangent-normal to 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q7:c — Both differentiate each parameter coordinate and form dy/dx. Q15(i) uses chain differentiation on cubic trigonometric coordinates and returns the general formula -tan t. The exam differentiates an exponential coordinate and the product 4t²e^(-t), then evaluates the quotient at the iterated parameter from (b) and rounds to 2 significant figures. The absent product-rule/exponential derivative and inherited numerical evaluation make the selected textbook subpart easier; its tangent/intercept follow-ups are excluded. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:parametric-fixed-parameter-line to 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q7:c — The retained source Goldie4E Q2(ii)-(iii) gives a fixed parameter and requires both tangent and normal equations. The target differentiate the supplied exponential parameter coordinates and use the previously iterated parameter to return a gradient to 2sf. The source adds two line-equation outputs and a negative-reciprocal slope; the target asks only for a gradient with its own trig/parameter conditions. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:parametric-geometric-point-selection to 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q7:c — The source derives its quotient, chooses a parameter from a positive y-axis crossing and obtains the point tangent. The target differentiate the supplied exponential parameter coordinates and use the previously iterated parameter to return a gradient to 2sf. Source axis/sign selection and line construction are additional, while the target has a prescribed/iterated parameter and different derivatives. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:parametric-prescribed-gradient to 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q7:c — The source Goldie4E Q11 derives the quotient, sets it equal to the prescribed slope one and solves for a point using the original coordinates/domain t>1. The target differentiate the supplied exponential parameter coordinates and use the previously iterated parameter to return a gradient to 2sf. Searching a parameter from a slope differs from selecting axis crossings or evaluating at an already known parameter. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:explicit-root-arc-investigation to 8227:EXAM:2021:P2:Q5:a — Only the selected derivative responses are compared: the source differentiates x times a square root of a quadratic, needing product rule and a nontrivial inner chain. The exam differentiates x ln(x)-x, whose product-rule constants cancel. The source adds the inner-root chain; intercept, maximum and endpoint-gradient responses are excluded. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:numerical-supplied-map-chain to 8227:EXAM:2022:P2:Q5:a — The source establishes a supplied map from a curve-intersection equation. The exam must first differentiate x times an exponential and impose the prescribed gradient 20 before isolating the logarithmic map; this calculus model is absent from the selected source response. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:numerical-gradient-constraint-chain to 8227:EXAM:2022:P2:Q5:a — The source uses exactly the same curve 6x exp(x/3), with gradient 40 instead of 20, then also brackets and iterates. The isolated exam part asks only for the derivative/gradient rearrangement; the exact source (a) exception excludes the extra numerical responses. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:gradient-point-evaluation to 8227:EXAM:2022:P2:Q3:b — Example7 differentiates the explicit polynomial product (3x-2)(x+4) and evaluates its gradient at a fully supplied point. The target must differentiate 2sin^2(x)+7sin(x)+3 and evaluate its exact gradient at the previously selected axis crossing. The source is easier: it omits trig-chain differentiation and exact special-angle evaluation. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:direct-time-rate to 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q11:c — Example19 differentiates a supplied cubic volume-time formula and evaluates its signed rate at a stated time. The target uses exponential decay and explicitly requests a positive rate of decrease. The source is easier power-sum time differentiation; exponential differentiation and decrease-sign interpretation are additional target decisions. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:connected-rate-inverse to 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q11:c — The book a-gradient differentiates a supplied explicit curve and evaluates its gradient at a given x. The exam differentiates a time-decay model and interprets the signed derivative as a positive rate of decrease with units. The rate interpretation is absent from the selected source gradient response. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:stationary-classify-sketch to 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q5:a — The source locates cubic extrema, classifies and sketches them. The exam uses the logarithmic quotient 5x/ln(x) and returns exact stationary coordinates without classification or drawing. The source adds outputs; the exam adds quotient/log differentiation and a logarithmic stationary equation. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:stationary-classify-sketch to 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q10:a — The source locates both cubic extrema and classifies/sketches them. The exam differentiates two logarithms and solves for one admissible stationary abscissa. The source adds ordinate/nature/drawing responses; the exam adds logarithmic domains and rational-denominator algebra. (mixed).

Demands without an NSSCAS part: none.

### Difficulty and grade descriptors

E13names elementary standard/composite derivatives;C13/A14retain them with product/quotient and implicit/parametric extensions. Harder nesting changes chain/domain reasoning:√xversus√(e^x),ln(x²)versus(lnx)²,and powers ofsinxversussin(kx). Numericalcoefficient size alone is not a levelboundary.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

Goldie9andYM14have a valid derivative but a loglawworking route losing negativex. YM16(d)prints a wrongnumerator coefficient. Degreecoordinates require theangularunitfactor. These are source/author cautions,not examiner-frequency evidence or justification to classify a standard derivative by the sophistication of its modelsolution.

### Gaps and representations

Both books provide rich symboliccalculus;Goldie’s derivativegraph frame adds a representation not equivalent to formula-only practice. Optionalproofs and reciprocal-function derivations remain labelledsupport. Originalholes and finite-derivative endpoints remain even when a simplifiedanswer looks defined elsewhere.

### Proposals for step 3

Review problem sets for these exact demands: 8227:2020:M2:T4:O01:D01, 8227:2020:M2:T4:O01:D02.

Begin equivalence review with 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:elementary-composite-derivative against 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q4:a; 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:implicit-given-point-gradient against 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q5:answer; 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:parametric-fixed-parameter-gradient against 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q7:c; 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:numerical-gradient-constraint-chain against 8227:EXAM:2022:P2:Q5:a. Carry only the selected exemplar response labels into later branch-reference decisions.

A concrete easier comparison is 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:product-polynomial-radical-derivative against 8227:EXAM:2021:P2:Q5:a: The source applies the product rule to two algebraic factors and simplifies. The exam applies it to x ln(x), also differentiates the subtracted x and recognises cancellation. The source omits the logarithmic derivative and the additive cancellation. Use this difference when deciding scaffolding and connected responses; the complete ordered comparison list is in the objective map.

A concrete harder comparison is 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:explicit-second-derivative-zero against 8227:EXAM:2021:P2:Q5:a: The exam asks only for the first derivative of xlnx-x, using product rule then cancellation. The source differentiates an explicit product twice, sets its second derivative to zero and returns original coordinates; those additional response decisions make this a harder branch. The exp versus log family alone is not the distinction. Use this difference when deciding scaffolding and connected responses; the complete ordered comparison list is in the objective map.

A concrete mixed comparison is 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:elementary-product-derivative against 8227:EXAM:2021:P2:Q5:a: Source Example 16(a) differentiates x ln(ln(x)), so its product term includes a nested logarithmic chain. The exam differentiates x ln(x)-x, where the product contribution cancels the subtracted derivative. The source adds a nested log and the exam adds cancellation; neither full response is identical. Use this difference when deciding scaffolding and connected responses; the complete ordered comparison list is in the objective map.

## Product/quotient selection, prescribed methods and valid alternatives

Demands: 8227:2020:M2:T4:O02:D01, 8227:2020:M2:T4:O02:D02.

### Teaching across the books

Goldie opens with product/quotient derivations and examples explicitly demanding those methods,then permits alternate productrewrite in laterexamples. YM shows expansion as a valid alternative for its opening polynomialproduct before adding affinepowers,radicals and elementaryfunctions. The same printed expression can have different assessed method evidence when one source enforces a rule and another permits any valid method.

(mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:parametric-prescribed-gradient to 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q7:c — The source Goldie4E Q11 derives the quotient, sets it equal to the prescribed slope one and solves for a point using the original coordinates/domain t>1. The target differentiate the supplied exponential parameter coordinates and use the previously iterated parameter to return a gradient to 2sf. Searching a parameter from a slope differs from selecting axis crossings or evaluating at an already known parameter. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:explicit-root-arc-investigation to 8227:EXAM:2021:P2:Q5:a — Only the selected derivative responses are compared: the source differentiates x times a square root of a quadratic, needing product rule and a nontrivial inner chain. The exam differentiates x ln(x)-x, whose product-rule constants cancel. The source adds the inner-root chain; intercept, maximum and endpoint-gradient responses are excluded. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:implicit-given-point-gradient to 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q8:a — Both differentiate an implicit relation using product/chain rules and isolate dy/dx. YM Example 29 additionally evaluates it at (1,2) to return -5/6, while 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q8:a stops at proving a supplied general gradient formula. The formula family and coefficients alone do not justify the branch; the extra numerical point evaluation does. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:numerical-supplied-map-chain to 8227:EXAM:2022:P2:Q5:a — The source establishes a supplied map from a curve-intersection equation. The exam must first differentiate x times an exponential and impose the prescribed gradient 20 before isolating the logarithmic map; this calculus model is absent from the selected source response. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:numerical-gradient-constraint-chain to 8227:EXAM:2022:P2:Q5:a — The source uses exactly the same curve 6x exp(x/3), with gradient 40 instead of 20, then also brackets and iterates. The isolated exam part asks only for the derivative/gradient rearrangement; the exact source (a) exception excludes the extra numerical responses. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:gradient-point-evaluation to 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q1:answer — Example7 differentiates the explicit polynomial product (3x-2)(x+4) and evaluates its gradient at a fully supplied point. The target must differentiate the affine rational function (2x-3)/(x-2) and evaluate its gradient at x=-4. The source is easier: it omits the variable denominator and quotient/reciprocal rewrite. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:stationary-classify-sketch to 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q5:a — The source locates cubic extrema, classifies and sketches them. The exam uses the logarithmic quotient 5x/ln(x) and returns exact stationary coordinates without classification or drawing. The source adds outputs; the exam adds quotient/log differentiation and a logarithmic stationary equation. (mixed).

Demands without an NSSCAS part: none.

### Difficulty and grade descriptors

C13/A14explicitly include products and quotients. Difficulty grows through innerchainfactors,radicaldomains,secondorder or geometrylater;presence of a fraction alone is insufficient. Rationalpowers/simplifiedsums inMaths1remain validlower scaffolds without requiringMaths2rules.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

YMTry8(c)’sWHOLEsqrtquotient has a validnegative-domaincomponent lost by splittingits key radicals. An equivalent quotient/productrewrite must retainoriginalpoles;correctincidence or one sampleinput doesnotcertify the derivative. Quotientorder and missingproductterm are mathematical feedback hypotheses here,not an asserted examinererrorrate.

### Gaps and representations

Both books offer direct drill breadth and applications. Goldie supplies explicitlyprescribed methodbranches;YM provides free-method expansion/rewrite contrasts. A derivative used to find a pointgradient or stationarycoordinate is a component unless the formula/method is independently asked. Sourcepartlinking must preserve that distinction.

A concrete easier comparison is 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:product-polynomial-radical-derivative against 8227:EXAM:2021:P2:Q5:a: The source applies the product rule to two algebraic factors and simplifies. The exam applies it to x ln(x), also differentiates the subtracted x and recognises cancellation. The source omits the logarithmic derivative and the additive cancellation. Use this difference when deciding scaffolding and connected responses; the complete ordered comparison list is in the objective map.

A concrete harder comparison is 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:explicit-second-derivative-zero against 8227:EXAM:2021:P2:Q5:a: The exam asks only for the first derivative of xlnx-x, using product rule then cancellation. The exam differentiates x ln(x)-x, where the product contribution cancels the subtracted derivative. The source adds a nested log and the exam adds cancellation; neither full response is identical. Use this difference when deciding scaffolding and connected responses; the complete ordered comparison list is in the objective map.

## Function meaning, domains and images

Demands: 8227:2020:M1:T3:O01:D01, 8227:2020:M1:T3:O01:D02, 8227:2020:M1:T3:O01:D03, 8227:2020:M1:T3:O02:D01, 8227:2020:M1:T3:O02:D02.

### Teaching across the books

YM compares graphs,tables,lists and mappings before notation,domain/image projections and formula-derived ranges. It explicitly separates function validity from one-to-one status. Its range progression moves from monotone affine functions to restricted quadratics with interior extrema and open endpoints. Goldie2&3uses functions and polynomial graphs as assumed background rather than providing a parallel introductory sequence;its retained algebra sketches cannot replace these definition/domain tasks.

### Examination comparison

Directly assessed NSSCAS parts: 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q12:a, 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q12:b, 8227:EXAM:2022:P1:Q6:a, 8227:EXAM:2022:P2:Q9:a, 8227:EXAM:2022:P2:Q9:b.ii, 8227:EXAM:2022:P2:Q9:c.i, 8227:EXAM:2022:P2:Q9:c.ii, 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q2:b, 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q6:b, 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q7:b, 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q7:c, 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q7:d.

(easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:function-injectivity to 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q7:b — The source applies the horizontal-line/colliding-output test to a fixed graph or arrow mapping and classifies it as one-to-one or many-to-one. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:function-given-graph-domain-image to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q6:b — Only the source range reading is compared: extrema and endpoint heights are already supplied on the graph and are projected onto the y axis. The exam must obtain the vertex and endpoint heights from its algebraic quadratic before deciding the attained image. The source omits that analytic feature construction. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:function-natural-domain-range to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q12:a — Only Try Now 12(b) domain is compared: its radical is in a denominator, so the input must make the radicand strictly positive. The exam has a numerator square root and admits its zero endpoint. The source adds the nonzero-denominator restriction; it also requests a separate range response. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:function-natural-domain-range to 8227:EXAM:2022:P1:Q6:a — The source domain for a reciprocal square root requires its affine radicand to be positive; the exam logarithm also requires a positive affine argument. The real-function reason differs (denominator/radical versus logarithm), and the full source item additionally asks its image. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:function-natural-domain-range to 8227:EXAM:2022:P2:Q9:a — The source enforces strict positivity inside a denominator square root, while the exam enforces it inside a base-2 logarithm. The affine threshold algebra is shared but the function-domain reason differs and the source also requests a range. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:function-natural-domain-range to 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q2:b — Source Example 12(a) derives the natural domain and unattainable output of one rational function. The exam asks only for the domain of its already obtained rational inverse. The source adds inversion/image reasoning; its domain-only exception has the same single-pole exclusion. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:function-affine-restricted-image to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q6:b — The source maps the two endpoints of a monotone affine rule and preserves open/closed inclusion. The quadratic exam interval straddles its vertex, so it must also test the interior extremum before comparing endpoint heights. The source is easier endpoint practice without that turning-point decision. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:function-affine-restricted-image to 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q7:c — The source affine image follows its monotone direction and an unbounded or finite stated input interval. The exam first uses the selected one-to-one quadratic branch and its vertex to obtain an unbounded downward image. The source avoids the quadratic vertex/branch decision. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:function-composition-admissibility to 8227:EXAM:2022:P2:Q9:c.ii — Only Try Now 14 Q3(b) is compared: compare the whole inner range with the outer domain and explain why the declared composition gf cannot be formed on its complete input domain. The exam asks why one particular input makes fg undefined. The source requires a global range-inclusion decision rather than the single intermediate-value counterexample. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:function-decomposition-and-restricted-composite to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q12:b — The source first completes the given composite square, recovers the unknown inner rule on its valid sign, and then inverts the resulting quadratic composite with its actual image domain. The exam receives one principal-root rule and squares it to obtain its inverse. The source adds reverse-composition recovery and a square-root branch choice. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:function-decomposition-and-restricted-composite to 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q7:d — The source full chain recovers an inner rule from a supplied outer rule/composite before inverting the actual restricted quadratic and reporting its inverse domain. The isolated exam part asks only for that inverse/domain step; the named source (c) exception excludes the earlier decomposition. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:function-piecewise-image-inverse to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q12:b — The source obtains ranges, reflects a given graph and inverts both linear and reciprocal pieces with their separate validity intervals. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:function-piecewise-image-inverse to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q6:b — Only source (a) is compared: it joins the images of two monotone pieces using their open/closed endpoints. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:function-piecewise-image-inverse to 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q7:c — Only source (a) is compared: it joins two finite piece images into one bounded interval. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:function-known-composite-inverse-chain to 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q7:d — Only the source composite inverse and its domain in (b) are compared. The source is harder by the preceding composition and does not inherit a direct-inverse same-type label. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:function-piecewise-sketch-domain-image to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q6:b — The source draws a quadratic piece followed by a line, reconciles their common input endpoint and reports the union domain/image. The exam must find a restricted quadratic image by checking the interior vertex and interval endpoints.
