(mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:function-piecewise-sketch-domain-image to 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q7:c — The source draws a quadratic piece followed by a line, reconciles their common input endpoint and reports the union domain/image. The exam applies it to ln(x-2) and requests the single numerical cutoff 2. Demands without an NSSCAS part: none.

### Difficulty and grade descriptors

E03sets terminology and simple injectivity/inverses;C03/A03include domain/range identification and linked function work. Increased demand comes from endpoint attainment,interior extrema and propagated exclusions,not coefficient size. Full-circle relation projection is supporting representation practice,not a function on the whole circle.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

YMExample12prints a wrong pole sign and an impossible negative part of a root-function image;Example4has a sign typo in an intermediate header. Repeated outputs do not violate function validity;conflicting outputs for one input do. Distinguish textbook/source defects from examiner-confirmed learner confusion;ERR04is only broad corroboration for the linked domain issue.

### Gaps and representations

YM provides explicit multi-representation function definitions lacking in the retained Goldieprogression. A given graph and a worked solution graph have different roles;domain/range can be read from the former but must be derived from a formula in another family. A flag for graph use does not prove that the learner must draw it.

## Injectivity, restricted inverse rules and branch resets

Demands: 8227:2020:M1:T3:O03:D01, 8227:2020:M1:T3:O03:D02.

### Teaching across the books

YM moves from inverse algebra to reflection and then a quadratic branch restriction. It distinguishes a least right-hand endpoint from a greatest left-hand endpoint,and later resets a parameter for a new inverse. Its piecewise example maps each output interval to a separate inverse clause. Goldieassumes inverse/function background in its logarithm/trigonometry chapters;those later special-function methods broaden instances but do not supply a substitute forYM’s explicit branch-selection sequence.

The exam inverts one principal-root rule and gives one inverse domain. The source adds casewise algebra and interval assignment, rather than one square/swap operation. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:function-piecewise-image-inverse to 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q2:a — The source (c) inverts a linear piece and a reciprocal-affine piece and assigns each inverse its bounded input interval. The exam inverts one reciprocal-affine rule on its full nonpole domain. The reciprocal algebra is shared but the source adds a second rule and piece-interval allocation. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:function-known-composite-inverse-chain to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q6:c — Only the source composite inverse and its domain in (b) are compared. The source first constructs fg and then inverts that quadratic on x>=0; the target directly supplies its restricted quadratic. The source is harder by the preceding composition and does not inherit a direct-inverse same-type label. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:function-known-composite-inverse-chain to 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q7:d — Only the source composite inverse and its domain in (b) are compared. The source first constructs fg and then inverts that quadratic on x>=0; the target directly supplies its restricted quadratic. The source is harder by the preceding composition and does not inherit a direct-inverse same-type label. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:exponential-log-inverse-chain to 8227:EXAM:2022:P1:Q6:b — Source Example29 establishes the logarithm domain cutoff, derives its exponential inverse and sketches both curves. The target asks only for the inverse rule of a shifted logarithm after its earlier cutoff part. The source includes the domain/sketch responses in addition to the matched selected inverse. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:exponential-log-inverse-chain to 8227:EXAM:2022:P2:Q9:b.i — Source Example29 establishes the logarithm domain cutoff, derives its exponential inverse and sketches both curves. The target inverts ln(x-2), using an exponential and the opposite operation order. Both invert the exp/ln pair, but the free parameters and direction of algebra differ. (mixed).

Demands without an NSSCAS part: none.

### Difficulty and grade descriptors

E03includes simple inverse decisions;C03/A03permit linked branch/domain work. A restricted quadratic needs a sign decision justified by the original domain. Piecewise inverse intervals and parameter-dependent ranges are harder through logical conditions rather than larger constants. QUAL08requires preserving the branch actually used.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

YMExample10asks an inverse at-2although its inverse input must be nonnegative;Try10Q2(c)omits the original quadratic branch. These are registered source defects. A formally rearranged squared expression is not a valid inverse everywhere. Try13Q2also illustrates why a later explicit reset must override the preliminary extremal bound.

### Gaps and representations

YM provides abundant quadratic restrictions and some piecewise practice. The sampled exams include rational and logarithmic inverse forms as well;later special-function chapters must supply that breadth. A textbook error is retained as internal caution,not an approved defective learner prompt.

## Composition order, decomposition and whole-domain admissibility

Demands: 8227:2020:M1:T3:O04:D01, 8227:2020:M1:T3:O04:D02.

### Teaching across the books

YM explicitly teaches pointwise operations before ordered composition,evaluation before symbolic formation,and then equations,missing rules and full-domain containment. Try9Q5uses commuting inverse affine rules as a counterexample to an unqualified never-commutes claim. Try14Q3asks why one order fails on a declared domain even though the algebraic expression exists for some inputs. Goldie’s later chained standard functions give context for these methods,but its retained corpus lacks the same introductory decomposition sequence.

### Examination comparison

Directly assessed NSSCAS parts: 8227:EXAM:2022:P2:Q9:c.i, 8227:EXAM:2022:P2:Q9:c.ii, 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q2:c.i, 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q2:c.ii.

Same-type textbook practice: 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:function-compose-evaluate to 8227:EXAM:2022:P2:Q9:c.i; 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:function-form-composition to 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q2:c.i.

Structural branches: 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:function-composite-equations to 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q2:c.ii — The source forms a reciprocal-affine composite and solves its specified-output equation for an unknown input x, checking the excluded pole. The exam has a known input and solves its specified-output equation for a coefficient a appearing in the rules. The unknown role differs and the source adds an admissibility check. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:function-composition-admissibility to 8227:EXAM:2022:P2:Q9:c.ii — Only Try Now 14 Q3(b) is compared: compare the whole inner range with the outer domain and explain why the declared composition gf cannot be formed on its complete input domain. The exam asks why one particular input makes fg undefined. The source requires a global range-inclusion decision rather than the single intermediate-value counterexample. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:function-decomposition-and-restricted-composite to 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q2:c.i — The source must recover an unknown inner g from known f and fg, including the allowed square-root sign. The exam simply substitutes one supplied rule into another to form gf. Reverse rule recovery and sign/domain checking make the source broader than forward composition. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:function-known-composite-inverse-chain to 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q2:c.i — Only the a-gf response is compared. The source composes two known polynomial functions and simplifies; the target substitutes a reciprocal function into a linear rule with an unknown additive parameter. The source is easier fixed-coefficient polynomial substitution; its other order, inverse and equation are excluded. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:function-composite-coefficient-recovery to 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q2:c.ii — Only Exercise3.1 Q4(b) is compared: form two opposite-order composite conditions and solve for positive a,b. The target uses one previously formed composite and solves a single linear coefficient equation. The source is harder in correlated equations, squared values and positive-branch selection; its preceding range/injectivity task is excluded. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:same-gradient-curve-translation-support to 8227:EXAM:2022:P2:Q9:c.ii — The source identifies a logarithm’s valid input interval. The exam first evaluates the inner linear function g(1)=1, then uses f’s x>3 restriction to explain why fg(1) is undefined. The composition input test is an additional decision and explanatory response. (easier).

Demands without an NSSCAS part: none.

### Difficulty and grade descriptors

C03/A03explicitly include forming composites and recognising decomposition. The structural increase is from evaluating a supplied pair to recovering an unknown rule or proving image containment. A reciprocal pole and a branch reset can matter even when symbolic expressions simplify to polynomials.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

YMExample7has a wrong nested-function step beside correct numeric outputs;Try14Q1’s inverse domain isSTRICTx>10. Revision3Q2(c)includes degeneratek=0in its no-solution set. A discriminant-only author solution can miss the degree-losing case. These are individually checked source/author cautions;no specific examiner-frequency claim is inferred.

### Gaps and representations

The books/exams emphasise different directions:known-rule substitution is common,missing-rule and whole-domain admissibility are extraYMbreadth. Multiplying outputs is a different operation. One valid sample input does not establish a composition on the complete declared domain.

## Specified-domain sketches, inverse overlays and transformation investigations

Demands: 8227:2020:M1:T3:O05:D01, 8227:2020:M1:T3:O05:D02.

### Teaching across the books

YM contrasts an asked original sketch,an inverse overlay on a supplied graph and a shared function/inverse diagram. Its six transformation investigations explicitly compare input/output shifts,reflections and reciprocal horizontal scales,including square-root domain changes. Goldie’s retained polynomial sketches provide end-behaviour/crossing practice,but not the same systematic inverse-reflection introduction. Later exponential/trigonometric chapters add special-function graph forms.

Demands without an NSSCAS part: none.

### Difficulty and grade descriptors

C03/A03name specified-domain original and inverse graphs. Difficulty grows through branch endpoints,asymptotes,piecewise images and coupled transformations. An extra guide line is a representation aid,not necessarily an additional assessed function. This synthesis proposes feature categories only;branch sketch marking is step3.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

Horizontal input shifts have opposite coordinate direction and horizontal scales are reciprocal. Those are textbook-derived teaching cautions. ERR04supports the general inverse/domain/graph link,but does not establish how frequently each transformation mistake occurs. The full graph must agree with its stated interval,including endpoint markers.

### Gaps and representations

YM has structured transformations and affine/restricted quadratic inverse pairs;the exam’s logarithm asymptote requires additional sources later in thebook. Given image,worked solution image and requested learner image are separately recorded. No app capability hold is introduced:the owner’s diagram/sketch support decision remains binding.

## Translate geometric graph constraints into equations

Demands: 8227:2020:M1:T4:O01:D01.

### Teaching across the books

YM teaches line/perpendicular midpoint constructions,then shape recovery,distance/gradient parameters,reflection and linked area/ratio tasks. Some diagrams provide data;others illustrate the worked solution. Goldie’s vectors chapter develops displacement and scalar-product geometry as a different method family;its retained graphs can support coordinate reasoning without replacing the explicit line-equation tasks inYM.

### Examination comparison

Directly assessed NSSCAS parts: 8227:EXAM:2022:P1:Q14:a, 8227:EXAM:2022:P1:Q7:answer, 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q8:answer, 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q11:a, 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q12:a, 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q12:b, 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q4:b.

Same-type textbook practice: 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trig-shared-graph-deductions to 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q4:b; 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:coordinate-perpendicular-bisector to 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q12:b; 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:area-negative-and-sign-split to 8227:EXAM:2022:P1:Q14:a.

Structural branches: 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:coordinate-perpendicular-bisector to 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q12:a — Only source Example18(a) is compared: find a segment midpoint and negative-reciprocal gradient, then write its perpendicular bisector. The exam additionally identifies the rhombus diagonal BD as that bisector from its shape properties. The source names the required bisector explicitly, omitting this property-selection step. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:coordinate-perpendicular-bisector to 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q12:b — The source whole rhombus chain first constructs its perpendicular bisector, then finds the y-axis vertex and opposite vertex, then computes area. The exam part inherits the line from (a) and asks only for those two coordinates. The full source adds line construction and area; the b-only same-type exception excludes both. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:coordinate-perpendicular-bisector to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q8:answer — The source constructs a perpendicular bisector through a given segment midpoint, intersects another line and returns a distance. The exam instead projects a point onto an axis determined by two points, then uses the projection as the original/reflection midpoint. The source adds distance calculation; the exam adds projection/point-reflection reasoning. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:coordinate-parallel-perpendicular-shape to 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q12:a — The source chooses parallel/perpendicular directions from a trapezium and derives two side lines before locating their intersection. The exam chooses perpendicular bisecting diagonals of a rhombus and writes one diagonal through its midpoint. The source adds side intersection, while the exam adds midpoint/bisection use instead of a supplied point on each side. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:coordinate-parallel-perpendicular-shape to 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q12:b — The source derives side lines from a trapezium and intersects them for one missing vertex. The exam uses a preceding diagonal to locate an axis vertex and its opposite via the known diagonal midpoint. The source adds two line constructions; the exam adds the bisection recovery of a second vertex. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:coordinate-parallel-perpendicular-shape to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q8:answer — The source uses shape-implied parallel/perpendicular side lines to recover a vertex by intersection. The exam derives an axis and a perpendicular projection, then uses midpoint reflection to recover the final point. The source requires parallel-side selection; the exam requires reflection after the foot is found. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:coordinate-reflection to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q8:answer — The source receives the reflection-axis equation and one point, obtains its perpendicular foot and reflects by the midpoint relation. The exam must first obtain the axis equation from A and C, then performs those same projection/reflection steps. The source is easier because its axis equation is supplied. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:coordinate-basic-line-and-distance to 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q12:a — Only Try Now16(a) is compared. The source explicitly supplies the midpoint and perpendicularity construction, while the target requires the learner to infer it from a rhombus diagonal. The source is easier stated-construction practice; its axis point and distance in (b) are excluded. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:explicit-tangent-incidence-parameter to 8227:EXAM:2022:P1:Q7:answer — The source imposes that the tangent at unknown p to (4-x)*exp(x) passes through the origin, equating its derivative with a secant/incidence slope. The target instead supplies tangent slope -9 and recovers the quartic contact point/intercept. Unknown contact is shared, but point-incidence versus prescribed-slope equations and product-exponential versus polynomial differentiation differ. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:modulus-equation-to-sketch to 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q11:a — The source constructs a line, its reflected absolute-value graph and a vertical translate on separate axes. The target sketch a symbolic modulus line and a signed straight line on shared axes. The source prepares the basic V/vertex transformation, but not the target shared pair and parameter coordinates. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:modulus-parameter-shared-graphs to 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q11:a — The book selection requires two reflected V graphs with a positive symbolic parameter and a shared intersection pattern. The exam sketches one V graph and an unreflected straight line with numerical coefficients. The second absolute-value reflection and symbolic positioning are extra decisions; later inequality answers are excluded. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:modulus-quartic-root-count to 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q11:a — Both produce shared-axis modulus-and-other-function sketches. The exam uses a supplied modulus-line pair with a positive parameter and does not ask for a root-count proof in (a); the textbook chooses the pair from its equation and justifies exactly two intersections. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:quadratic-modulus-shared-condition to 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q11:a — Both include shared-axis modulus graph work. The source must first establish parameter bounds for a quadratic and then add that parabola against |x|; the exam sketches an affine line against an affine V. The source adds quadratic/root-sign reasoning. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:power-sum-differentiation to 8227:EXAM:2022:P1:Q7:answer — Example1 returns single-term power derivatives (including roots, reciprocals and a constant). The target must additionally use prescribed tangent slope -9 to find an unknown quartic contact and line intercept. The drill is easier derivative-rule preparation; it omits that application/condition and response. (easier).

Demands without an NSSCAS part: none.

### Difficulty and grade descriptors

E04andA04both connect graphs with equations;their broad statement does not prescribe numeric coordinate ranges. Difficulty varies by whether the crucial line/property is supplied or inferred,and by whether two valid parameter positions must be retained. These tasks also use explicit prior coordinate-geometry components;do not claim every distance calculation is a newASdemand.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

YMExercise3.2Q3has a vertical-gradient exception;Try19Q1/Q2have two correlated point branches;Revision3Q3expressly requests3dpinstead of the usual3sf. Example24only gives one diagonal bisection plus perpendicularity,so inventing a rhombus changes its constraints. These are source/author cautions;no dedicated examiner-error rate for them is claimed.

### Gaps and representations

YM provides many shape/line chains;Goldie supplies alternative vector methods elsewhere. Supplied two-line intersection is less than inferring the lines from a diagram. An area or ratio final output requires evidence beyond a correct missing vertex. Both books’ conventions remain subordinate to the original question and official awards.

The exam instead projects a point onto an axis determined by two points, then uses the projection as the original/reflection midpoint. The source adds distance calculation; the exam adds projection/point-reflection reasoning. Use this difference when deciding scaffolding and connected responses; the complete ordered comparison list is in the objective map.

## Graph intersections, tangency and parameter degeneration

Demands: 8227:2020:M1:T4:O02:D01, 8227:2020:M1:T4:O02:D02.

### Teaching across the books

YM C1introduces substitution for numeric intersections and discriminant contact conditions;C3Example22adds a given intersection root,other-root recovery,tangency parameter and contact point. Try22uses a reciprocal curve,no-intersection interval,then actual intersections and their bisector. Goldie’s later tangent calculus provides another route in its proper context,but it does not make derivative calculation mandatory in an algebraic repeated-root task.

### Examination comparison

Directly assessed NSSCAS parts: 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q10:a, 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q11:b, 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q8:b, 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q11:a, 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q4:c.

Same-type textbook practice: 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:line-curve-no-intersection-parameter to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q10:a; 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trig-shared-graph-deductions to 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q4:c; 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:exponential-logarithmic-solve to 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q8:b.

Structural branches: 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:quadratic-nature-without-solving to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q10:a — The source classifies fixed numerical coefficients; the exam first eliminates a line against a curve and solves a discriminant inequality for all non-intersecting slopes. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:quadratic-parameter-conditions to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q10:a — The source starts with one quadratic and a root-count condition; the exam must construct that quadratic by eliminating the line against the curve before finding the slope interval. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:quadratic-inequality to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q10:a — The source starts with a quadratic inequality; the exam must eliminate a line against a curve and infer the discriminant condition for no intersection before solving it. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:line-curve-numeric-intersection to 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q11:b — The source substitutes a line into xy+18=0, solves a quadratic and returns both coordinate pairs. The exam intersects a line with an absolute-value graph, chooses the consistent branch and returns one symbolic pair. The source adds quadratic/two-pair recovery; the exam adds modulus-branch validity and a symbolic parameter. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:line-curve-numeric-intersection to 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q11:a — Equate the two ordinate expressions and rearrange to prove the supplied polynomial condition on intersection abscissas. The exam also clears x^2 from a rational curve, keeping x!=0; source a has polynomial/line data with no denominator restriction. Later solving, tangency and area are excluded. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:line-curve-parameter-contact to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q10:a — Both eliminate the line and use a discriminant inequality to select parameter values. The source requires two distinct intersections (positive discriminant, exterior parameter intervals); the exam requires no intersection (negative discriminant, an interior interval). These are different intersection-count branches of the same method, with no intrinsic total difficulty order; their required sign/endpoint decisions differ. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:line-curve-no-intersection-parameter to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q10:a — Example33 puts its unknown only in the curve constant term, so the negative-discriminant condition reduces to a linear parameter inequality. The exam parameter lies in the linear coefficient and gives a quadratic parameter inequality with two endpoints. This member is the easier inequality-solving branch and does not inherit the Try Now33 Q4 same-type match. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:line-curve-universal-meeting-proof to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q10:a — The source eliminates the line/curve system and proves its discriminant is (3k+1)^2, so meeting occurs for every real parameter including tangency. The exam solves the strict negative-discriminant condition to exclude all intersections. A universal nonnegative-square proof replaces a negative-inequality interval; these evidence requirements are different and not intrinsically ordered. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:line-curve-at-least-one-intersection to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q10:a — Both substitute the line and use a quadratic discriminant to delimit parameter values. The circle source supplies the discriminant hint and asks for meeting, including tangency; the exam requires choosing nonintersection and strict inequality for a parabola. Algebraic setup and the strictness/choice decisions point in different directions, so mixed is warranted. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:quadratic-completed-square-inequality-interval to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q10:a — Try Now23(c) can reuse the established completed-square form to isolate (x-3)^2<16 and return one open interval. The target must derive a parameter inequality from a line missing a quadratic curve. The source is easier scaffolded inner-interval practice; the target additionally forms a parameter/discriminant inequality. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:exponential-continuous-context-solve to 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q8:b — The source colony example sketches, evaluates its exponential population and translates a target population into a log/time calculation. The target equate those two exponential curves and find their exact intersection abscissa. The source adds contextual interpretation and a multiresponse chain; the target uses a different prior-result/two-curve equation. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:modulus-single-constant-equation to 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q11:b — The source has a fixed positive right side and two direct linear branches. The target find symbolic intersection coordinates of a modulus graph and a signed straight line, so its variable signed right side needs a nonnegativity/branch check and back-substitution for the ordinate. The source omits those decisions. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:modulus-variable-rhs-equation to 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q11:b — Both solve a modulus-linear intersection with an original-side sign check. The exam retains a positive symbolic parameter and asks for both coordinates after a shared sketch; the exemplar solves a numerical scalar equation. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:modulus-two-equation to 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q11:b — The source compares two absolute values, so both sides are automatically nonnegative and squaring/equal-opposite branches are valid. The target has one modulus against a signed linear expression and must keep its nonnegative-side restriction before returning intersection coordinates. That sign validity and requested response differ from the source two-modulus equation. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:modulus-nonlinear-inner-equation to 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q11:b — The source opens |x^3-14|=13 and then recovers real cubic preimages of the two intermediate values. The target find symbolic intersection coordinates of a modulus graph and a signed straight line. Cubic preimage recovery replaces the target linear, exponential or graph-coordinate response, so this is a different nonlinear-inner branch. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:modulus-linear-system to 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q11:b — The source solves 3x-2y=13 with |x+y|=1 and returns correlated coordinate pairs by substitution/back-substitution. The target likewise solves a line/modulus intersection but its coefficients/bounds contain positive a and only one branch survives its signed line. Numeric two-pair recovery and symbolic branch filtering differ. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:vector-retained-cartesian-intersection-support to 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q11:b — The source finds the intersection of two ordinary lines supplied by endpoint pairs, so it must obtain their gradients and intercepts. The exam has a supplied line and a modulus graph in terms of a and must choose the valid modulus branch before solving. The source adds line construction; the exam adds branch consistency. (mixed).

Demands without an NSSCAS part: 8227:2020:M1:T4:O02:D02.

### Difficulty and grade descriptors

E04/C04explicitly connect intersections to equations and simple tangency to repeated roots. Harder cases are original poles,nonintersection intervals,linear degeneration and further geometric reuse. Larger coordinates alone do not boundgrade. GradeA04supports graph/equation relationships without importing unrestricted conic intersections.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

Original denominators remain excluded after clearing them. The geometric meaning of discriminantzero requires a genuine quadratic intersection equation;ERR05corroborates the danger of blindly applying discriminants outside that condition. A complete point requires recovering both coordinates,not just roots inx.

### Gaps and representations

YM supplies numerical,parameter,contact-point and later-bisector branches;Goldie’s calculus context supplies a different method emphasis later. A topic tag does not make every intersection equally scaffolded. Root-count proofs and domain restrictions must stay explicit across the chain.

## Implicit differentiation, regular slopes and original-curve geometry

Demands: 8227:2020:M2:T4:O03:D01.

### Teaching across the books

Goldie explicitly differentiatesdependent-ybuildingblocks before completeimplicitcurves andgivenpoint/geometrictasks. YMseparates polynomial,log,trigandexponentialrelations,thenpointgradients,horizontalpoints andlines. Some relations can be rearranged explicitly first;that validalternative is retained where not prohibited. Mere placement ofy=x²primerinimplicitexposition doesnotmake it an implicitproblem.

### Examination comparison

Directly assessed NSSCAS parts: 8227:EXAM:2021:P2:Q10:a, 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q8:a, 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q5:answer.

Same-type textbook practice: 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:implicit-curve-derivative to 8227:EXAM:2021:P2:Q10:a; 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:implicit-linked-normal-curve-sketch to 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q8:a; 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:implicit-given-point-gradient to 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q5:answer.

Structural branches: 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:implicit-dependent-expression to 8227:EXAM:2021:P2:Q10:a — Goldie differentiates separate expressions xy, 3x²y³ and sin y with y dependent on x, retaining dy/dx in each output. 8227:EXAM:2021:P2:Q10:a differentiates a full relation and collects/isolates dy/dx; it must establish the supplied general derivative formula. The elementary implicit-expression practice is a scaffold, not coverage of the complete curve response. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:implicit-dependent-expression to 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q8:a — Goldie differentiates separate expressions xy, 3x²y³ and sin y with y dependent on x, retaining dy/dx in each output. 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q8:a differentiates a full relation and collects/isolates dy/dx; it must establish the supplied general derivative formula. The elementary implicit-expression practice is a scaffold, not coverage of the complete curve response. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:implicit-dependent-expression to 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q5:answer — Goldie differentiates separate expressions xy, 3x²y³ and sin y with y dependent on x, retaining dy/dx in each output. 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q5:answer differentiates a full relation and collects/isolates dy/dx; it then substitutes the given point for a numerical gradient. The elementary implicit-expression practice is a scaffold, not coverage of the complete curve response. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:implicit-curve-derivative to 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q8:a — The source Example25 implicitly differentiates polynomial terms and xy. The target additionally differentiates y^3*sin(2x), requiring the trig chain factor 2 and a product between dependent and direct-variable functions. Polynomial implicit collection is easier supporting practice. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:implicit-curve-derivative to 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q5:answer — The source Example28 derives an implicit exponential-product expression but stops at the general derivative. The target uses one exponential-times-y product plus y^2 and additionally evaluates its gradient at a supplied point. The source has a second product and a y^3 term; the target adds numerical point evaluation. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:implicit-point-gradient-line to 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q5:answer — The source GoldieExample16 derives the implicit gradient of y^3+xy=2, evaluates it at a fully given point and constructs a tangent. The target differentiate 2exp(3x)*y+y^2=15 implicitly and evaluate its gradient at the supplied point (0,-5). The source adds its tangent/derivative-expression response, while the target uses an exponential product but asks for gradient only. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:implicit-linked-normal-curve-sketch to 8227:EXAM:2021:P2:Q10:a — The source Goldie4D Q6 derives the implicit gradient of (x-6)(y+4)=2, constructs a normal at (7,-2), finds its second curve intersection and obtains asymptotes/sketch. The target establish the implicit derivative of the polynomial relation 3xy-5y-2x^2=6. The source adds connected line/intersection/representation responses. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:implicit-linked-normal-curve-sketch to 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q5:answer — Both differentiate an implicit product and isolate dy/dx. Q6(i) has the simpler affine product (x-6)(y+4)=2 and returns a general expression. The exam product 2e^(3x) y also needs exponential chain differentiation, includes y², and requires evaluation at (0,-5). Thus the named derivative subpart is an easier branch. The gradient calculation embedded in ii supports transfer, but the asked normal line is not silently treated as a gradient-only response; ii–iv are excluded. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:implicit-given-point-gradient to 8227:EXAM:2021:P2:Q10:a — Both differentiate an implicit relation using product/chain rules and isolate dy/dx. YM Example 29 additionally evaluates it at (1,2) to return -5/6, while 8227:EXAM:2021:P2:Q10:a stops at proving a supplied general gradient formula. The formula family and coefficients alone do not justify the branch; the extra numerical point evaluation does. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:implicit-given-point-gradient to 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q8:a — Both differentiate an implicit relation using product/chain rules and isolate dy/dx. YM Example 29 additionally evaluates it at (1,2) to return -5/6, while 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q8:a stops at proving a supplied general gradient formula. The formula family and coefficients alone do not justify the branch; the extra numerical point evaluation does. (harder).

A14explicitlynames finding/using implicitderivatives;C13/E13supply standardandproduct/quotientfoundations. The difficultboundary isregularity,not coefficient size:theimplicitdenominatorcanvanish,and0/0needsbranchanalysis. Formalfirst-principlesproofremainsoutside requiredscope.

Goldie17’sorigin is singular andneeds a qualification;YM27’sx0componentmustnot beassigned afiniteratio. Splittingln(xy)into ln x+lnycanlose the negative-negativebranch;implicitproducttermsneedboth contributions. These are registeredsource/author cautions,no examiner-frequencyclaims.

Bothbooks supply routine andelementaryimplicitcurves. Goldie adds generalparameteruniqueness andlogarithmicvariable-exponentwork;YM explicitgivenpointchecks make regularity inspectable. Optionalx^xlogroute doesnotturn allvariable-powerexpressions into constantpowerrules.

### Difficulty and grade descriptors

A14includesparametric/implicitderivativeuse;C13suppliesrulefoundation. Harderbranches involveX′=0versusY′=0,both-zero stalls/cusps,parameterorientation andoriginalangle/logdomains. Generalintercepts require nonzeroscale assumptions and unsignedareacorrectness. No grade-specificparameterdomains are decided here.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

YMTry39Q2’swronglinehascorrectincidencebutwrongslope;Try42Q1missescoordinatesandcalls aminimumamaximumafter omittinganX-chainfactor. Goldie4E6prints2abwithouttheunsignedabsolutevaluequalification;4E4omitsa≠0. Somealgebraicallycancelledstationaryparametersarecusps rather than regularhorizontalpoints.

### Gaps and representations

Goldie offers moregeneralparameterline/interceptproofs,YM moregivenellipse-label/fixedprecisiontargets. Aplottedparametricpathwithoutanyderivativeaskis supportingrepresentationpractice,not directderivativecoverage. Negativeparameterneednotmeaninvalidnegativetimewhen tissimplyarealparameter.

## Standard exponentials, reciprocal-linear and affine trig primitives

Demands: 8227:2020:M2:T5:O01:D01, 8227:2020:M2:T5:O01:D02.

### Teaching across the books

GoldieC5teaches affine exponentials/reciprocals then standard trig forms;YMC12separates exponential,log-validityand trig sections before exact finite evaluation. YM explicitly asks ordinary-ln validity in6/Try6; Goldie explanations and the independent answer checks retain absolute-log primitives on both real components.

Demands without an NSSCAS part: none.

### Difficulty and grade descriptors

A09/C15bound the listed affine forms; general substitution is not required. The entry branch compensates one inner coefficient; harder originals require algebraic expansion,correct ordinary-log branch or a requested single-log final form. E09/C09supply reverse differentiation foundations.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

YM8(b)crosses a pole and its finite endpoint answer is invalid. A wholly negative denominator interval such asTry8(b)is valid with absolute logs. Negative inner slopes flip primitive signs; finite root or reciprocal endpoints must be checked before evaluation.

### Gaps and representations

YM has more explicit validity/answer-form drills; Goldie has more mixed source contexts. General nonlinear-log integrals inGoldieC8remain extra even when their answer is verified. Both books contain valid alternative forms that differ by a constant.

### Proposals for step 3

Review problem sets for these exact demands: 8227:2020:M2:T5:O01:D01, 8227:2020:M2:T5:O01:D02.

Begin equivalence review with 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:elementary-exponential-primitives against 8227:EXAM:2021:P2:Q9:a; 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:elementary-reciprocal-definite-logform against 8227:EXAM:2021:P2:Q2:answer; 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trig-square-power-reduction-integral against 8227:EXAM:2021:P2:Q9:b; 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:log-derivative-division-integral-chain against 8227:EXAM:2021:P2:Q2:answer; 1 further named same-type family/part pairs are in the objective map. Carry only the selected exemplar response labels into later branch-reference decisions.

A concrete easier comparison is 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:elementary-exponential-primitives against 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q9:b: The same affine exponential reverse derivative is needed, but the exam first interprets a positive velocity as distance rate and evaluates bounds 0 to 2 in metres; the family returns an indefinite primitive with C. Use this difference when deciding scaffolding and connected responses; the complete ordered comparison list is in the objective map.

A concrete harder comparison is 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:log-derivative-division-integral-chain against 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q5:b: Both reuse a polynomial quotient/remainder to separate polynomial and reciprocal-linear terms. The source then proves an exact definite value using endpoints; the exam asks for an indefinite primitive with C and the original denominator hole, following its prior division part. Use this difference when deciding scaffolding and connected responses; the complete ordered comparison list is in the objective map.

A concrete mixed comparison is 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:elementary-proper-exponential-integrals against 8227:EXAM:2021:P2:Q9:a: The source evaluates a proper finite exponential integral, including both endpoints. The exam instead simplifies an exponential quotient to 4exp(-3x)-2 and gives an indefinite primitive with a constant. The source adds endpoint evaluation; the exam adds quotient simplification and its polynomial term. Use this difference when deciding scaffolding and connected responses; the complete ordered comparison list is in the objective map.

## Supplied reverse-derivative scaffolds and method boundaries

Demands: 8227:2020:M2:T5:O01:D03.

### Teaching across the books

Goldie8Activity2explicitly supplies products to differentiate and rearrange before integrating;YM7Example12andC12Example17/Try17derive a suitable reciprocal derivative before reverse use. Those scaffolds make the route different from asking the learner to invent general parts/substitution. A derivative-at-point alone does not automatically supply the required primitive.

### Examination comparison

Directly assessed NSSCAS parts: 8227:EXAM:2021:P2:Q5:b, 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q4:b, 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q10:b.

The exam inherits the derivative of tan^2(x), recognises a half-derivative pair hidden in three terms, integrates the remaining tan^2(x) by identity and evaluates exact bounds. The source adds fresh differentiation; the target adds grouped recognition, trig identity and endpoints. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trig-derivative-hence-integrals to 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q10:b — The source derives tan(2x), evaluates a sec^2(2x) integral, chooses the tan^2 identity and uses a supplied double-angle reciprocal rewrite. These are related identity/derived-primitive chains, with the differing decisions retained. The exam instead inherits a tan^2 derivative and must recognise tan+tan^3 as half of it before treating its remaining tan^2 term. Both reuse derived trig primitives, but the source adds its own proofs/derivative whereas the target hides a different grouping. Neither is uniformly harder. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:secant-derived-identity-integral-chain to 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q10:b — The source proves secant identities, differentiates reciprocal cosine and combines the derived secant-tangent and known secant-square primitives. The exam instead inherits a tan^2 derivative and must recognise tan+tan^3 as half of it before treating its remaining tan^2 term. Both reuse derived trig primitives, but the source adds its own proofs/derivative whereas the target hides a different grouping. Neither is uniformly harder. The source rational derivative has a polynomial inner derivative; the target has an exponential inner derivative. The source is easier algebraic chain reversal and does not practise the exponential factor. (easier).

Demands without an NSSCAS part: none.

### Difficulty and grade descriptors

E09/C09/A09bound reverse differentiation; general substitution is not required. Easier branches match a supplied derivative directly; harder ones require scaling/rearrangement and a remaining standard primitive. Keep the actual supplied information and hence edge as difficulty boundaries.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

Goldie8Prompt4blanket claim about2/(x squared-1)is false under its already taught extra methods; the key only rejects one substitution. A printed method claim is source evidence to audit,not an unquestionable rule. CAS additive constants do not make two equivalent primitives conflicting answers.

### Gaps and representations

Goldie has explicit parts motivation and many forced methods outside8227; YM stays closer to its listed forms but still uses reverse-derived sec/cot primitives. Source method compliance and mathematical validity are separate axes.

## Identity choice, squared trig expressions and geometric integration

Demands: 8227:2020:M2:T5:O02:D01, 8227:2020:M2:T5:O02:D02.

### Teaching across the books

Goldie5Bdevelops square reduction,compound products and scaffolded higher-power forms;YM14/16/23separates freely chosen identities from prove-then-hence and complementary-integral deduction. Goldie8unforced finite trig products can be reduced by derived identities; optional substitution in its solution does not make the original extra-scope.

### Examination comparison

Directly assessed NSSCAS parts: 8227:EXAM:2021:P2:Q9:b, 8227:EXAM:2022:P2:Q3:c, 8227:EXAM:2022:P2:Q7:b, 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q3:b.iii, 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q5:b, 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q6:a, 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q6:b, 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q10:b.

Those are additional target decisions. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:elementary-trig-definite to 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q5:b — The source evaluates exact endpoints of an already linear sine/cosine sum. The exam additionally chooses both sin^2 and tan^2 identities before it can use the standard primitives. The source is easier exact endpoint practice and does not demonstrate those identity choices. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:elementary-trig-definite to 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q6:a — The source evaluates exact endpoints of an already linear sine/cosine sum. Demands without an NSSCAS part: none.

### Difficulty and grade descriptors

C14/C15/A09explicitly name identities to facilitate squared-trig integration. Entry tasks use one half-angle replacement; harder sources expand a mixed square,repeat reduction or derive a finite triple identity. The derived identity is not an extra memorisation requirement.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

Expand the whole square including its cross term and double the original inner argument. YM24(a)has equivalent primitives differing by a constant; the checker now fixes a deterministic independent integration algorithm. Rotation requires the squared whole radius and actual original region.

### Gaps and representations

YM provides repeated exact,henceand area/volume chains; Goldie has wider optional/forced-method contrasts. Repeated source question occurrences are not extra family breadth. Diagram boundaries or requested maxima may end a region before its first intercept.

Only the standard reverse derivative is shared; identity transfer/cancellation is absent from the source. ## Trapezium ordinates, weights, precision and tabulated applications

Demands: 8227:2020:M2:T5:O03:D01, 8227:2020:M2:T5:O03:D02.

### Teaching across the books

GoldieC5explicitly explores many widths,individual constituent trapezia,piestimation and tabulated train motion. YMC12first contrasts the exactx squared area with a four-strip estimate,then uses prescribed two/three/four intervals and literal decimal/significant-figure outputs. A common-log node evaluation is not a change-of-base question.

### Examination comparison

Directly assessed NSSCAS parts: 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q6:a, 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q6:b, 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q4:a.

Same-type textbook practice: 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trapezium-prescribed-estimate to 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q6:a; 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trapezium-prescribed-estimate to 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q4:a; 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trapezium-graph-bias-chain to 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q4:a; 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trapezium-graph-bias-chain to 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q6:a; 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trapezium-stationary-preparation to 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q6:a; 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trapezium-stationary-preparation to 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q4:a; 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trapezium-oscillation-aliasing to 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q6:a.

The exam additionally recognises x^2-4x+4=(x-2)^2 and scales an approximation, while the source directly presents ln(x^3) and rescales an exact value. The unselected earlier geometric derivation is context. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trapezium-graph-bias-chain to 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q6:a — The source estimates an integral with a prescribed strip count and then explains its error direction from the curve/chords. The isolated exam part asks only for the numerical estimate; the source additionally assesses the geometric bias explanation. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trapezium-graph-bias-chain to 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q4:a — The source estimates an integral with a prescribed strip count and then explains its error direction from the curve/chords. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trapezium-stationary-preparation to 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q6:a — The source connected chain first finds the minimum input of x-2ln(x), computes a trapezium estimate and then reasons about its bias. The isolated exam part asks only for a trapezium estimate. A separate source (b) exception preserves the same numerical-integration task without inheriting the extra stationary/bias outputs. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trapezium-stationary-preparation to 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q4:a — The source connected chain first finds the minimum input of x-2ln(x), computes a trapezium estimate and then reasons about its bias. The isolated exam part asks only for a trapezium estimate. A separate source (b) exception preserves the same numerical-integration task without inheriting the extra stationary/bias outputs. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trapezium-complement-bias to 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q6:a — The source first estimates area A under sqrt(1+x^3), then obtains complementary area B by 6-A and reverses the error direction. The exam part assesses only the numerical trapezium estimate. Source complement/bias decisions add to that shared execution. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trapezium-complement-bias to 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q4:a — The source first estimates area A under sqrt(1+x^3), then obtains complementary area B by 6-A and reverses the error direction. The exam part assesses only the numerical trapezium estimate. Source complement/bias decisions add to that shared execution. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trapezium-refinement-limitations to 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q6:a — Goldie Q2 estimates pi from a given exact integral using four, ten and twenty strips, then justifies confidence in digits. The exam part requests one prescribed-strip estimate. Repeated refinement and justified accuracy are additional source decisions. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trapezium-refinement-limitations to 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q4:a — Goldie Q2 estimates pi from a given exact integral using four, ten and twenty strips, then justifies confidence in digits. The exam part requests one prescribed-strip estimate. Repeated refinement and justified accuracy are additional source decisions. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trapezium-oscillation-aliasing to 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q4:a — The source evaluates four nested trapezium grids for 2-cos(2pi*x), sketches their approximating strips and compares with an exact integral. The exam part asks for a single numerical estimate. Aliasing across grids, visual chord placement and exact comparison are extra source responses. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trapezium-tabulated-motion to 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q6:a — The source obtains distance from supplied time-speed ordinates and gives two reasons for numerical inaccuracy. The exam computes its own ordinates from a symbolic integrand and only requests the estimate. Source table reading is easier than ordinate evaluation, but its accuracy reasons and motion units are additional responses. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trapezium-tabulated-motion to 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q4:a — The source obtains distance from supplied time-speed ordinates and gives two reasons for numerical inaccuracy. The exam computes its own ordinates from a symbolic integrand and only requests the estimate. Source table reading is easier than ordinate evaluation, but its accuracy reasons and motion units are additional responses. (mixed).

Demands without an NSSCAS part: none.

### Difficulty and grade descriptors

A10/C15bound trapezium applications; begin with given ordinates then derive nodes for a stated interval count. Harder tasks add data units,complementary area,required final answer form or multiple refinements. More strips alone are not a higher Bloom classification.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

nintervals needn+1ordinates and doubled interior weights. Keep internal precision before final rounding;YM24Tryasks2sf0.90,not a display that loses the trailing zero. Exact values and estimates must retain different labels.

### Gaps and representations

Goldie has richer refinement/data/model-limitation work;YMoffers many exam-style given curves. Goldie5C6shows coarse-node aliasing,so stable repeated estimates do not establish correctness. The train table lacks complete between-sample behaviour.

## Integrand sketches and justified trapezium bias

Demands: 8227:2020:M2:T5:O04:D01, 8227:2020:M2:T5:O04:D02.

### Teaching across the books

Goldie links repeated sketches,straight-top approximations and a mixed-curvature no-universal-direction example;its closing investigation checks left/right/midpoint claims with counterexamples. YM distinguishes supplied diagrams from required sketches,and asks chord-position explanations after numeric estimates. The actual finite integration interval matters more than the full natural-domain graph.

### Examination comparison

Directly assessed NSSCAS parts: 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q6:c, 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q6:d, 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q4:b.

Same-type textbook practice: 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trapezium-graph-bias-chain to 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q6:d; 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trapezium-graph-bias-chain to 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q4:b; 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trapezium-given-graph-bias-only to 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q6:d; 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:exact-integral-sketch-bias-without-estimate to 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q6:d; 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:exponential-log-formula-sketch to 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q6:c.

Structural branches: 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:reciprocal-sign-and-graph to 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q4:b — The source sketches the full-cycle cosecant from sine zeros and extrema. The exam sketches its positive first-quadrant branch and then compares the curve with trapezium chords to justify an over-estimate. The source supports reciprocal graph production but omits the chord/area-bias argument. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:reciprocal-graph-uniqueness to 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q4:b — Both require the positive first-quadrant cosecant branch, but the source adds a line and uses intersections to justify one root. The exam instead compares the cosecant curve with trapezium chords to justify integral bias. Neither inference substitutes for the other; only the reciprocal-graph subtask transfers. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trapezium-graph-bias-chain to 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q6:d — The source estimates the exp(x)cos(x) area then uses the supplied concave-down graph to justify an underestimate. The exam part requires only the bias explanation from its preceding logarithmic sketch. The source adds numerical estimation; the selected bias-only exception is separate. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trapezium-graph-bias-chain to 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q4:b — Source Example24(b) includes the same cosecant sketch and overestimate explanation as the exam, plus the separate numerical trapezium estimate in (b)(i). Only the explicitly selected sketch/bias responses carry same-type equivalence. The source printed word concave is a registered defect and is not adopted. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trapezium-complement-bias to 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q6:d — The source must estimate A, subtract from a fixed rectangle to obtain B, and reverse the overestimate of A into an underestimate of B. The exam instead explains the error of the under-curve estimate itself from an already drawn logarithmic curve. Complement subtraction and error reversal differ from the target. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trapezium-complement-bias to 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q4:b — The source must estimate A, subtract from a fixed rectangle to obtain B, and reverse the overestimate of A into an underestimate of B. The exam instead explains the error of the under-curve estimate itself using its own required cosecant sketch. Complement subtraction and error reversal differ from the target; the target also requires producing the sketch. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trapezium-given-graph-bias-only to 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q6:d — The source classifies three supplied curves, including a changing-curvature case where bias cannot be decided. The exam explains only one concave-down logarithmic case. Extra case discrimination makes the complete source harder; its concave-down (i) alone has a named same-type exception. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trapezium-given-graph-bias-only to 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q4:b — The source supplies three graphs and requires bias classification, including an indeterminate changing-curvature case. The exam instead requires producing its own cosecant sketch before explaining the overestimate. The source adds case discrimination but supplies the graphs; neither whole task is uniformly harder. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:trapezium-given-graph-bias-only to 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q4:b — Selected supplied convex graph (iii) supports the chords-above/overestimate judgement. The target additionally requires the learner to construct the cosecant sketch and its domain; this is easier supporting bias practice, not the complete target sketch response. The isolated exam part sketches a translated logarithm, including a vertical asymptote and intercept. Extra source outputs and the different graph-construction decisions prevent equivalence. Demands without an NSSCAS part: none.

### Difficulty and grade descriptors

C15explicitly uses simple sketch graphs for over/under conclusions;A10includes trapezium applications. Start with a single-concavity finite interval; harder sources involve complement bias reversal,changing curvature or aliasing. General universal error theorems are not invented scope.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

An asymptote outside the integration interval does not invalidate the proper estimate. A maximum alone does not decide chord bias. Area-complement subtraction reverses error direction. A drawing without a reason is not a complete explanation.

### Gaps and representations

Goldie supplies uncertainty/counterexample variety;YMhas clear estimate-sketch-bias chains and source cue defects. Worksheet plots,required learner sketches and given diagrams must remain distinct. Left/right/midpoint enrichment is not automatically an8227requirement.

## Index/log definitions, laws and original-domain equivalence

Demands: 8227:2020:M2:T2:O01:D01, 8227:2020:M2:T2:O01:D02.

### Teaching across the books

YM explicitly converts notation in both directions before exact evaluation,expansion/compression and solving. Goldie begins from exponential graphs/common-base reasoning and physical logarithmic-scale contexts. Both expand and compress within a base;neither route authorises requiring the excluded change-of-base theorem. YM6’sprinted positive-factor expansions can be narrower than the original squared-product domain;the inventory records both the source convention and absolute-log extension.

Demands without an NSSCAS part: none.

### Difficulty and grade descriptors

E11/C11/A12include logarithm-index relation and laws. Difficulty changes through original domain,variable base,correlated simultaneous pairs and target form;it is not determined by whether thebaseis10ore. The parenthetical exclusion of change of base constrains required knowledge,not a ban on taking common logs to solve a general exponential equation.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

YMExercise9Q1keyuses the wrong variable;Example10has no real root after filtering;Exercise9Q12retains a forbidden-2kkeyroot. Goldie2B13has a defective physical earthquake model even though its arithmetic follows the supplied formula. Textbook/source defects are not learner-error statistics.

### Gaps and representations

Both books offer conversion and laws;YMsystematically contrasts validnegativevariableswithinvalidarguments. Textbook scale discussions need explicit physical assumptions. Formula compression must not silently enlarge the admitted domain of separate original logarithms.

(mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:exponential-graph-root-count to 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q12:a — Both draw a curve pair and use intersections to justify a real root count. Source Example26 pairs exp(x) with a line and has one crossing; the target pairs ln(x) with both branches of a modulus line and must establish exactly two. Modulus branch handling and the second crossing are additional target decisions. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:exponential-natural-inverse-subject to 8227:EXAM:2022:P1:Q6:b — The source makes t the subject of s=s0*exp(-kt), requiring isolation, a natural logarithm and division by nonzero k. The target inverts ln(x-2), using an exponential and the opposite operation order. Both invert the exp/ln pair, but the free parameters and direction of algebra differ. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:exponential-log-formula-sketch to 8227:EXAM:2022:P1:Q6:c — The source explains vertical/horizontal translation and stretch of the exponential base graph. The target sketches a function and its inverse together, preserving the admitted branch and swapped intercepts. The source has no paired inverse sketch, while its four transformation cases add decisions beyond the one given inverse pair. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:numerical-graph-count-bracket to 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q12:a — Only Example2(a) graph/root-count response is compared. The source chooses and sketches exponential and straight-line graphs; the target has a two-branch modulus and a positive-domain logarithm. The source is easier smooth-curve comparison; its numerical bracket is excluded. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:modulus-equation-to-sketch to 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q12:a — The source constructs a line, its reflected absolute-value graph and a vertical translate on separate axes. The target sketch a modulus line and a logarithm and justify exactly two roots. The source prepares the basic V/vertex transformation, but not the target shared pair and global intersection-count argument. (easier).

Demands without an NSSCAS part: none.

### Difficulty and grade descriptors

E11/C11/A12includee^x,lnx,inverse relation and graphs. Harder cases are shiftedasymptotes,restrictedclosedendpoints,reflections aboutdifferentaxesandconnectedcompositions. Gradeboundsdo not justify formal substitution as a new required integration technique.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

YMTry29Q3wrongly prints the range forclosedx≥0;Exercise9Q14draws an inverse where an output reflection was asked;Exercise9Q6(a)omits a necessaryquadraticbranch. Graphanchorslabelled2.7or3are approximatevalues,not exactequalities. These source defects remain internal and cannot define valid variants.

### Gaps and representations

Both books provide graph transformations;YM has substantial software investigations and a given-graph base recovery task. A finite viewport is not a globalroot-count proof. Given,solution and response figures stay separate. The owner-approved sketch capabilities are recorded as requirements,not new capability holds.

## Exponential solves, positive substitutions and inequalities

Demands: 8227:2020:M2:T2:O03:D01, 8227:2020:M2:T2:O03:D02.

### Teaching across the books

Both books progress fromone-base solves tomixed-affine exponents,positiveexponentialsubstitutionandreciprocal forms. YMexplicitly asks allpositive intermediatevalues before recoveringx;Goldie addscontextualcontinuous times,half-life anddiscreteGPindices. They also contrast increasing/reciprocal bases in inequalities. Source methods such as trial/improvement or an expressly requested logbase remain relevant.

The exam selects the first T_n below a bound directly from a*r^(n-1). Both require logarithms/integer boundary checks, but the source adds the sum-model isolation before that stage. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:progression-context-modelling to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q4:b — Example33 translates percentage-decay donations into a GP and returns a first-year threshold, two calendar-block totals and a cumulative-total threshold. Demands without an NSSCAS part: none.

### Difficulty and grade descriptors

E11/C11/A12include exponential equations and similar inequalities. Difficulty grows through positivityfiltering,twopositivebranches,exactformconstraints and orderreversal. A positive intermediate below1gives a validnegativeexponent. Whole-calendar/term selection is extra contextual interpretation,not automatic rounding of everytime answer.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

GoldieExample4has a wrongfactoredsolution;YMExercise9Q12illustrates originaldomain rejection in a relatedlogcase. Approximatecutoffs3.11or-2.30must not replaceexactboundaries. Squared/absolute finalsubstitutions can add original±branches,andnegativeintermediatesmust be rejected. Source errors are separate from evidenced learner mistakes.

### Gaps and representations

Both books offer broadalgebraic solving;Goldiecontextmodels addunitsandtimestep distinctions. YM3sfdecimals coexist with exactkln3or-in-einstructions. Agraphroot-count proofand a numeric solve requiredifferent responses. Empty/all-real inequalitycases stay deliberate where warranted.

## Linearising relationships, reading constants and fitting measurements

Demands: 8227:2020:M2:T2:O04:D01, 8227:2020:M2:T2:O04:D02.

### Teaching across the books

Goldie developsrawmeasurements,modeltests,transformedtables/plots,parameterestimates and predictionvalidity;it uses powerand exponentialcontexts. YMfirst explicitly chooses transformedaxes,thenrecoversconstantsfromgivenlinepoints,beforefits to experimenttables. Both usebase10andnatural logs;themodel/axischoice,not a genericlineformula,determines how gradient/intercept map back.

### Examination comparison

Directly assessed NSSCAS parts: 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q4:a, 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q6:a.

Same-type textbook practice: 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:logarithm-line-constants to 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q4:a.

Structural branches: 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:logarithm-linearise-model to 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q4:a — Goldie2C Q3 first proves the linear log(N)-against-t relation, then estimates its two independent model constants from a plotted line. The target recover A and p from a given ln(y)-against-x line for y=A exp(px+p). The source adds a contextual derivation/estimation chain, while the target couples its constant to the exponent offset or logarithmic gradient. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:logarithm-linearise-model to 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q6:a — Goldie2C Q3 first proves the linear log(N)-against-t relation, then estimates its two independent model constants from a plotted line. The target linearise log_3(y) against x and use its given gradient to recover the exponential base parameter. The source adds a contextual derivation/estimation chain, while the target couples its constant to the exponent offset or logarithmic gradient. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:logarithm-line-constants to 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q6:a — Linearise the exponential model, identify its logarithmic slope with the base parameter and recover the base. The book must first calculate that slope from two supplied transformed points; the exam directly states the base3 slope. Identifying the prefactor as an x-independent intercept is needed in both even when its parameter is unknown. The additional source gradient-from-points decision makes the selected book response harder; its separate A recovery is excluded. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:logarithm-data-model-fit to 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q4:a — The source Example7 starts with raw diameter/flow data, chooses log-log coordinates, plots a fitted line and estimates its power-law parameters. The target recover A and p from a given ln(y)-against-x line for y=A exp(px+p). The source adds data transformation and fitting uncertainty; the target gives transformed-line information and its own coupled exponential parameter algebra. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:logarithm-data-model-fit to 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q6:a — The source Example7 starts with raw diameter/flow data, chooses log-log coordinates, plots a fitted line and estimates its power-law parameters. The target linearise log_3(y) against x and use its given gradient to recover the exponential base parameter. The source adds data transformation and fitting uncertainty; the target gives transformed-line information and its own coupled exponential parameter algebra. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:exponential-affine-line-intersection to 8227:EXAM:2025:P2:Q6:a — The source takes logs of 3^y=4^(x+2), obtains an ordinary y-against-x slope and intersects that line with y=2x. The target takes log_3(y) of an exponential model and uses its specified gradient to recover a base parameter. The transformed ordinate and required inverse-parameter response differ, while the source additionally asks for a second-line intersection. (mixed).

Demands without an NSSCAS part: none.

### Difficulty and grade descriptors

A12explicitly includes transformedlinearrelationships and constantsfromgradient/intercept. E11/C11do not restate thatfull demand;they provide log foundations. Difficult cases aretranslatedexponents,negative slopes,B^(lnx),reversedaxes anduncertaingraph fits. This isqualitativecalibration,not a settled grade-specificparameterdomain.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

GoldieExample7’sworkedaxes reverse the requested order. YM33’schosenline givesb2.00,not1.20;Try33Q2’spositiveintercept supports pabout3.1,not.0123. Exercise9Q11omits the axes,so itsnumericanswers stayconditional. Earlierfalseleads inYMTry32Q2andTry33Q1werecleared on native pages;do not reinstate them asdefects.

### Gaps and representations

Goldie suppliesmoremodel-testing/prediction discussion;YM suppliesmoreexplicitaxis-modelalgebra and exactgivenpoints. Both have graphicalestimates that require reasonabletolerance rather than a single printednumber. A clinical/biological contextdoesnot establish causal effectiveness;the source stoppingcondition must constrain extrapolation.

A concrete easier comparison is 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:product-polynomial-radical-derivative against 8227:EXAM:2022:P2:Q5:a: The source supplies explicit polynomial factors and asks only for their product derivative. The exam product includes an exponential and must then impose a gradient and rearrange it logarithmically. This is easier product-rule support, not the complete fixed-point derivation. Use this difference when deciding scaffolding and connected responses; the complete ordered comparison list is in the objective map.

A concrete harder comparison is 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:numerical-rearrangement-branch-proof against 8227:EXAM:2021:P2:Q8:b: Goldie6B Q2 requires four fixed-point rearrangements of x^4+x-exp(x)=0, including a fourth-root and a square-root/division form whose real branch/domain must be retained. The target requires just the selected positive square-root rearrangement of a cubic. The source is harder in the number of transformations and branch/domain conditions. Use this difference when deciding scaffolding and connected responses; the complete ordered comparison list is in the objective map.

A concrete mixed comparison is 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:explicit-second-derivative-zero against 8227:EXAM:2022:P2:Q5:a: Both differentiate an explicit x-times-exponential curve and impose a derivative condition. The source differentiates twice, solves zero second derivative and returns original coordinates. The exam differentiates once, sets the gradient to20 and must rearrange to the prescribed p=3ln(10/(p+3)) form. Source derivative order/coordinate recovery and exam logarithmic rearrangement make a mixed branch; no arbitrary difference-count rejection is used. Use this difference when deciding scaffolding and connected responses; the complete ordered comparison list is in the objective map.

## Division, quotient/remainder and scope boundaries

Demands: 8227:2020:M2:T1:O03:D01, 8227:2020:M2:T1:O03:D02, 8227:2020:M2:T1:O03:D03.

### Teaching across the books

Goldie begins with aligned arithmetic, then cubic/linear division and several equivalent worked routes;its1A set moves from linear to quadratic divisors. YM separates linear and quadratic division and explicitly includes zero columns, nonmonic divisors and other variable letters. YMExample15 only asks a remainder although its solution works a quotient;Example16 asks both. GoldiePrompt3 asks why a reduced remainder has lower degree. These are different required outputs, not interchangeable answer fields.

### Examination comparison

Directly assessed NSSCAS parts: 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q1:a, 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q5:a.

Same-type textbook practice: 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:polynomial-linear-division to 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q1:a; 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:polynomial-linear-division to 8227:EXAM:2024:P2:Q5:a.

Structural branches: 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:polynomial-linear-division to 8227:EXAM:2023:P2:Q1:a — This member asks only for the scalar remainder; its worked quotient is supporting solution material. The exam explicitly requires the quotient as well as the shown remainder. ### Difficulty and grade descriptors

GradeA11 explicitly permits degree-at-most-four dividends and linear/quadratic divisors. It does not impose that dividend limit on unrelated multiplication or theorem evaluation. Nonmonic scaling, sparse columns and linear versus scalar remainder are meaningful difficulty branches;larger coefficients alone are not. No numeric grade parameter domain is set here.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

YMExample14 changes its dividend constant in a later displayed identity;Try15 omits requested quotients in the key. These source defects are recorded. Independent reconstruction P=DQ+R and degree checks can catch both arithmetic and wrong-output mistakes. The textbook cautions do not establish an examiner-reported rate of those errors.

### Gaps and representations

Both books provide substantial forward division practice;Goldie has explicit conceptual remainder-degree support. A remainder-only theorem exercise does not cover asked quotient recovery, and a surrounding quadratic-division heading does not makeYMTry16Q2(c) a quadratic-divisor question. Supporting high-power arithmetic must not create out-of-scope division coverage.

## Theorem evaluation, inverse constraints and factorisation

Demands: 8227:2020:M2:T1:O04:D01, 8227:2020:M2:T1:O04:D02, 8227:2020:M2:T1:O04:D03, 8227:2020:M2:T1:O04:D04.

### Teaching across the books

Goldie moves from value tables and integer-candidate tests to factor/solve/sketch chains, then inverse coefficient constraints. YM explicitly contrasts quotient/remainder computation with evaluation without division, then factor proofs, complete factorisation and polynomial solves. Both use nonmonic divisor roots exactly. YM19Q1 adds an unknown exponent;20Q2 uses an unspecifiedR and2R;Goldie1B14/YM23Q3 yield multiple parameter tuples. These are authored structural branches of one constraint-building decision, not a recipe per set of numbers.

Demands without an NSSCAS part: none.

### Difficulty and grade descriptors

GradeE10 names single-unknown linear theorem work;C10 broadens coefficient evaluation and polynomial solving;A11 names combined factor/remainder constraints. Nonlinear parameter tuples, parameter-dependent divisor roots and original-variable recovery increase reasoning demands. The title of a harder exercise is not evidence of a higher assessed Bloom level or a grade assigned here.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

GoldieExample9 demonstrates that no integer root is not no real root. YMExercise8 needs both distinct roots to prove a quadratic factor. Try27 has a wrong |x| recovery;Exercise18 needs secant/cosine range rejection. ERR10 supports original-variable recovery broadly. Wrong printed factors or copied signs are source defects, not demonstrated learner misconceptions.

### Gaps and representations

Both books provide inverse constraints and factor/solve chains;Goldie adds a richer factor-to-sketch progression, whileYM supplies exponent,composition and trigonometric recovery. Root multiplicity, irreducible real factors and complete original-domain checking need to remain visible. No absence from these textbook families changes the138-demand syllabus map.
