## Recognition, term/sum constraints and contextual progression models

Demands: 8227:2020:M1:T2:O02:D01, 8227:2020:M1:T2:O02:D02, 8227:2020:M1:T2:O03:D01, 8227:2020:M1:T2:O03:D02, 8227:2020:M1:T2:O03:D03.

### Teaching across the books

YM first classifies lists,then makes unknown terms AP/GP,before direct terms,index membership,finite sums,inverse counts and mixed contexts. It contrasts NEXTblocks with initial blocks and decreasing with increasing sequences. Goldie2&3assumes these Mathematics1skills;the retained corpus has no comparable standalone AP/GPexercise progression. Incidental geometric series in an unrelated derivation would be component support,not equivalent direct practice.

### Examination comparison

Directly assessed NSSCAS parts: 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q8:a, 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q8:b, 8227:EXAM:2022:P1:Q5:a, 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q5:a, 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q4:a, 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q4:b, 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q5:a, 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q5:b.

Same-type textbook practice: 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:ap-finite-sum to 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q5:b; 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:gp-term-threshold to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q4:b; 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:gp-finite-sums to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q4:a; 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:progression-context-modelling to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q8:a; 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:progression-context-modelling to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q4:b; 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:ap-parameters-from-sum-conditions to 8227:EXAM:2022:P1:Q5:a; 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:gp-terms-to-ap-finite-sum to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q4:a.

Structural branches: 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:exponential-inequality to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q4:b — The source solves a supplied decreasing-base real exponential inequality. The exam must construct the GP term inequality with its n-1 index, use the earlier ratio and choose the first admissible integer index. The source omits model construction and integer minimality. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:exponential-scale-context-support to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q4:a — The source applies a stated doubling ratio to obtain a later term; the exam instead recovers the unknown ratio r from a=145 and fourth term74.24 via r³=74.24/145. These are forward versus inverse uses of a discrete geometric model: an easier source branch, not the same requested response. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:exponential-scale-context-support to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q4:b — Both locate a count boundary in a discrete geometric model. The textbook prompt asks approximately how many doubling days produce a million cells; its page says to use powers of 2, so trial/powers are valid and logarithms optional. The exam asks the first operation whose extracted amount is strictly below 10, with term exponent n-1 and decay ratio from (a). Strict first crossing and indexing add exam conditions; growth versus decay changes inequality direction. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:exponential-scale-context-support to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q4:b — The selected source counts distinct audible pitches in a closed frequency window across seven octave-doubling sequences. The exam finds the first index below a strict bound in one geometric decay sequence. Both require admissible discrete indices from exponential bounds; the source has multiple sequences/two endpoints and duplicate-octave bookkeeping while the exam has first-crossing strictness and n-1 indexing. This is a mixed member-level branch. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:exponential-scale-context-support to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q4:a — The selected octave response applies the given factor2 three times to obtain a later frequency. The exam recovers an unknown GP ratio from the first and fourth terms by a cube root. Forward known-ratio evaluation versus inverse-ratio recovery is a genuine easier source branch, rather than a same-type formula substitution. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:progression-recognition to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q8:a — Example1 checks differences/ratios of listed terms and classifies AP versus GP. The target additionally recognise percentage-growth donations, count the inclusive years and sum the finite GP in money. The source is easier finite-term recognition; it omits the required cumulative-sum or inverse-coefficient response. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:progression-recognition to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q8:b — Example1 checks differences/ratios of listed terms and classifies AP versus GP. The target additionally recover an AP donation increment so its known-year total equals the preceding GP total. The source is easier finite-term recognition; it omits the required cumulative-sum or inverse-coefficient response. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:progression-recognition to 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q5:a — Example1 checks differences/ratios of listed terms and classifies AP versus GP. The target additionally prove from a general nth-term expression that its successive-term ratio is independent of n. The source is easier finite-term recognition; it omits a symbolic argument for all n. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:ap-term-and-membership to 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q5:a — The source reads an ordinary AP first term/difference and evaluates specified terms. The exam must first compute two GP infinite totals, recognise those as consecutive AP terms and extrapolate the third. The source supports the final AP term step only. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:ap-finite-sum to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q8:b — The source forms and evaluates an AP partial sum with a/d/n known. The exam instead uses that sum relation with its total known to solve for d and connects it to the preceding GP total. The source supports forming the sum but omits inversion and the connected equality. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:ap-finite-sum to 8227:EXAM:2022:P1:Q5:a — The source evaluates one AP partial sum from known a/d. The exam uses a partial sum as one equation together with an indexed-term equation to recover both unknowns. This is supporting sum-formula practice, not the complete parameter-recovery task. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:gp-finite-sums to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q8:a — The source sums a fixed known number of GP terms from a numerical sequence. The exam must translate a 10-percent annual increase to its ratio, count 2010..2018 inclusively and round the money total to the nearest ten. The source lacks the date-count, percentage interpretation and required rounding unit. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:gp-finite-sums to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q4:a — The connected source Try Now24 Q3 first recovers r from its first/fourth terms, then sums ten terms. The isolated exam part asks only for r; the ratio-only exception excludes the source sum. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:gp-sum-threshold to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q4:b — The source selects the least n with S_n above a bound, so it must first form and rearrange the finite-sum expression containing 1-r^n. The exam selects the first T_n below a bound directly from a*r^(n-1). Both require logarithms/integer boundary checks, but the source adds the sum-model isolation before that stage. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:ap-gp-connected-sums to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q8:a — The source must compute a GP finite total and use it as the required AP total to recover an AP count. The isolated exam part only computes the GP total; the source adds a second progression and quadratic count inversion. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:ap-gp-connected-sums to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q8:b — Both connect a finite GP total to an equal AP total. The source unknown is the AP term count, requiring a quadratic count equation and integer filtering. The exam count and first term are fixed and the unknown is only its arithmetic increase, giving a linear parameter equation. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:gp-convergence-and-infinite to 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q5:a — The source identifies/justifies one convergent GP and sums it. The exam must sum two different GPs and connect the resulting totals as successive AP terms to recover a third. The source omits this second-sum/AP connection. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:progression-context-modelling to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q8:a — Example33 translates percentage-decay donations into a GP and returns a first-year threshold, two calendar-block totals and a cumulative-total threshold. The target recognise percentage-growth donations, count the inclusive years and sum the finite GP in money. The source is harder in the additional calendar-block and cumulative-sum decisions; the matching isolated source response is named separately. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:progression-context-modelling to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q4:b — Example33 translates percentage-decay donations into a GP and returns a first-year threshold, two calendar-block totals and a cumulative-total threshold. The target use the established positive ratio to find the first integer operation whose extraction is below a threshold. The source is harder in the additional calendar-block and cumulative-sum decisions; the matching isolated source response is named separately. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:progression-payment-accumulation to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q8:a — Example35 first places each annual payment on the interest timeline, computes the correct number of compounding periods and then sums the accumulated contributions. The target directly supplies each donation as a percentage-growth GP without reinvested payments. The source is harder in timeline/first compounded contribution formation; a GP sum alone does not establish that model. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:gp-rebound-distance to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q8:a — The source models an initial unpaired fall plus doubled rise/fall GP terms for a specified impact count. The exam models one donation per year. The source requires identifying paired versus unpaired travelled segments before the finite sum; this added path accounting makes it a harder modelling branch. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:ap-three-term-condition to 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q5:a — The source imposes equal differences on three parameterised terms, solves y and returns the first three AP terms. The exam first computes two GP infinite totals and then extrapolates the third AP term. The source adds a parameter equation; the exam adds two GP totals before the AP step. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:gp-three-term-condition to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q4:a — The source has three consecutive terms depending on one parameter, so it imposes T2^2=T1*T3 and solves/filter a quadratic before recovering the ratio. The exam gives first/fourth numeric values and recovers r from one cube-root equation. The source adds parameter consistency and root filtering. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:gp-infinite-sum-and-inverse-conditions to 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q5:a — The source evaluates one convergent GP infinite total from its indexed terms. The exam computes two GP totals and uses their AP relation to deduce a third. This is supporting infinite-sum practice only. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:ap-parameters-from-term-conditions to 8227:EXAM:2022:P1:Q5:a — The source recovers a and d from two indexed-term equations and then returns the first three terms. The exam replaces one term constraint with a partial-sum constraint and asks only for a/d. The source adds output terms; the exam adds the finite-sum model. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:ap-parameters-from-sum-conditions to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q8:b — The source recovers both a and d from two given block sums. The exam already gives a and count, and obtains the AP total from its preceding GP; it then solves only for d. The source adds an unknown and next-block/cumulative conversion, while the exam supplies the cross-progression total. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:ap-parameters-from-sum-conditions to 8227:EXAM:2022:P1:Q5:a — Example17 gives a first-block sum and the next-block sum, so it first aligns them as S6 and S12 before solving a/d. The exam has T3 and S8, requiring no next-block-to-cumulative conversion. This source is the harder block-alignment branch rather than the exact term-plus-sum exemplar. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:ap-sign-count-and-positive-block to 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q5:b — The source first locates the AP sign boundary, uses a positive/negative count ratio, and subtracts two partial sums to total only the positive block. The exam simply sums the first fifteen terms of its established AP. The source adds sign-count modelling and block selection before its sum. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:gp-index-and-membership to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q4:b — The source finds an exact integer term position from a prescribed GP value. The exam solves a strict inequality and must choose/check the first integer crossing. The source equality lacks the rounding-direction and predecessor minimality decisions. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:gp-parameters-from-indexed-terms to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q4:a — The source uses the fourth/seventh terms to recover both the ratio and unknown first term, then gives three terms. The exam supplies the first term directly and asks only for r from the fourth term. The source adds first-term reconstruction and three explicit term outputs. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:gp-ratio-from-two-term-sum to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q4:a — The source derives r+r^2 from a supplied T2+T3 relation, solves a quadratic and selects the stated positive root. The exam obtains r^3 from T4/T1 and takes its unique real cube root. The source adds a two-term sum equation, two algebraic candidates and sign filtering. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:gp-terms-to-ap-finite-sum to 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q5:a — The source derives a GP ratio/infinite total and constructs an AP from two GP terms, then sums that AP. The exam constructs the AP from two GP infinite totals and extrapolates its next term. Raw terms versus infinite totals play different connecting roles, and finite summation replaces next-term extrapolation. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:gp-terms-to-ap-finite-sum to 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q5:b — The source derives its GP parameters before using two nonadjacent AP terms to recover d and sum ten terms. The exam part inherits consecutive AP terms and sums fifteen. The source adds all GP preparation and the nonadjacent-index difference recovery. (harder).

Demands without an NSSCAS part: none.

### Difficulty and grade descriptors

E02names AP/GPrecognition and terms/sums;C02andA02retain progression problem solving. Harder tasks require integer selection,negative-ratio parity,correlated branches,calendar/block indexing or a model inferred from geometry. A large count alone is not harder reasoning. Do not infer that every word problem is gradeaor every direct term is gradee.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

YMTry8Q9omits a valid zero APbranch;Try13Q7omits a negative GPratio;Example32has incompatiblecm/mdata. Example35payment timing is ambiguous,andExample33requires inclusive years. These are recorded source limitations. Textbook-advised adjacent-index verification is useful,but this synthesis claims no examiner-error frequency for these particular defects.

### Gaps and representations

YM provides routine drills and contexts beyond the sampled exam structures;the retained Goldiebookoffers no equivalent standalone sequence. Direct term thresholds and cumulative sum thresholds need different data contracts. AP/GPclassification can overlap in constant cases,and zero cases need an explicit ratio convention. A supplied staircase figure tests reading the first exceptional riser,not merely substituting into an APformula.

## Sigma bounds and convergent infinite progressions

Demands: 8227:2020:M1:T2:O04:D01, 8227:2020:M1:T2:O04:D02, 8227:2020:M1:T2:O05:D01, 8227:2020:M1:T2:O05:D02.

### Teaching across the books

YM explicitly asks expansion of Sigma notation before evaluation,reverses listed series into Sigma form,and then varies lower bounds,unknown upper bounds and infinite exponent shifts. It compares finite totals with the infinite limit before inverse-parameter and contextual recurring-decimal/rebound tasks. The retained Goldiebookdoes not provide a parallel direct sequence;its numerical-limit exposition should not be relabelled as AP/GPcoverage.

### Examination comparison

Directly assessed NSSCAS parts: 8227:EXAM:2022:P1:Q5:b, 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q5:b, 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q5:c, 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q4:c, 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q5:a.

Structural branches: 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:ap-term-and-membership to 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q5:a — The source reads an ordinary AP first term/difference and evaluates specified terms. The exam must first compute two GP infinite totals, recognise those as consecutive AP terms and extrapolate the third. The source supports the final AP term step only. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:gp-convergence-and-infinite to 8227:EXAM:2022:P1:Q5:b — The source whole chain identifies the series type, justifies convergence and computes an infinite sum from explicit terms. The exam evaluates one index-shifted infinite Sigma sum, without separate type/reason outputs. The source adds explicit justification; the exam adds lower-bound/exponent decoding. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:gp-convergence-and-infinite to 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q5:b — The source first solves the allowed convergence domain and then an inverse infinite-sum equation. The exam part only asks for the strict convergence interval, with an affine ratio m-4. The source adds the sum equation and a parameter response. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:gp-convergence-and-infinite to 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q5:c — The source first determines |x|<1 for 1+x+x^2+..., then solves a known infinite total with constant first term. The exam first term also depends quadratically on m, so its inverse-sum equation has extra root/filter decisions. The source is an easier constant-first-term inverse-sum branch. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:gp-convergence-and-infinite to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q4:c — The source explicitly identifies the series as GP, justifies convergence and obtains its sum. The exam inherits r and simply obtains the infinite extraction total. The source adds type and convergence explanations. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:gp-convergence-and-infinite to 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q5:a — The source identifies/justifies one convergent GP and sums it. The exam must sum two different GPs and connect the resulting totals as successive AP terms to recover a third. The source omits this second-sum/AP connection. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:gp-rebound-distance to 8227:EXAM:2022:P1:Q5:b — The source infinite distance requires separating the first fall and doubling later geometric heights. The exam infinite Sigma requires extracting the correct first term at lower index 3. The source adds path pairing, while the exam adds nonstandard-bound/exponent decoding. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:gp-rebound-distance to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q4:c — The source derives a distance bound from the initial fall plus the infinite paired rebound legs. The exam adds each pump volume once and needs only one geometric total. The source adds the unpaired first leg and factor-two modelling. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:ap-three-term-condition to 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q5:a — The source imposes equal differences on three parameterised terms, solves y and returns the first three AP terms. The exam first computes two GP infinite totals and then extrapolates the third AP term. The source adds a parameter equation; the exam adds two GP totals before the AP step. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:gp-convergence-interval-only to 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q5:b — The source ratio is 2cos(x)/sqrt(3), so the convergence test requires a bounded trigonometric inequality and two angular endpoints. The exam ratio m-4 yields a simple affine interval. Both impose |r|<1; the source adds trigonometric inversion and interval filtering. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:gp-infinite-sum-and-inverse-conditions to 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q5:c — The source connected example recovers a positive algebraic parameter from its three GP terms and then evaluates the infinite sum. The exam instead gives the infinite sum and solves for m affecting both first term and ratio. The source uses a three-term consistency quadratic; the exam uses an inverse-sum quadratic plus convergence filtering. These reverse given/unknown roles require different evidence. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:gp-infinite-sum-and-inverse-conditions to 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q5:a — The source evaluates one convergent GP infinite total from its indexed terms. The exam computes two GP totals and uses their AP relation to deduce a third. This is supporting infinite-sum practice only. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:gp-parameters-from-infinite-total to 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q5:c — The source gives a constant first term and an infinite total, solves a/(1-r)=S for its ratio parameter and checks convergence. The exam first term also depends quadratically on m, producing a quadratic inverse-sum equation and multiple candidates to filter. The source is an easier linear inverse-sum branch. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:gp-terms-to-ap-finite-sum to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q4:c — The source full revision chain recovers a GP ratio, finds its infinite total, then uses its terms for an AP sum. The exam part inherits r and asks only for its infinite total. The source adds ratio recovery and the second progression. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:gp-terms-to-ap-finite-sum to 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q5:a — The source derives a GP ratio/infinite total and constructs an AP from two GP terms, then sums that AP. The exam constructs the AP from two GP infinite totals and extrapolates its next term. Raw terms versus infinite totals play different connecting roles, and finite summation replaces next-term extrapolation. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:gp-terms-to-ap-finite-sum to 8227:EXAM:2022:P1:Q5:b — The source full chain reconstructs r from GP term positions, obtains its infinite total, then uses GP terms for an AP finite sum. The exam has one infinite Sigma expression with a shifted lower bound and exponent. The source adds ratio recovery and an AP; the exam adds explicit Sigma first-term decoding. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:gp-terms-to-ap-finite-sum to 8227:EXAM:2022:P1:Q5:b — Only source b-infinite is compared: its a and r are already established and the source directly applies a/(1-r). The exam must additionally decode the first term at index 3 and its reciprocal-base ratio from the Sigma summand. This selected source response is the easier known-parameters infinite-sum stage. (easier).

Demands without an NSSCAS part: none.

### Difficulty and grade descriptors

E02includes Sigma;C02explicitly includes convergence and infinite sums. The hardest source branches involve parameter filtering,index shifts and two linked series rather than larger numerical terms. The boundary |r|=1must remain outside ordinary infinite GPsum use. No derivative convergence test or numerical fixed-point theorem is imported into this topic.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

YMExample30’s coefficient24is not its first term because the first exponent is1;Try31Q5requires filtering recovered values through convergence. Try28inequalities ask allinteger outcomes,not an unasked least one. Book recurring-decimal dot groupings need the render;the first rebound drop is unpaired. These are source/author cautions with no examiner-specific error-rate assertion.

### Gaps and representations

YM supplies strong indexing and convergence variety;Goldie’s retained content supplies no equivalent direct practice. Finite Sigma and infinite Sigma are separate validity cases;computing a total does not by itself demonstrate interpreting notation or justifying convergence. The original response requirements must remain visible.

## Coordinate frames, notation and geometric vector operations

Demands: 8227:2020:M1:T7:O01:D01, 8227:2020:M1:T7:O01:D02, 8227:2020:M1:T7:O02:D01, 8227:2020:M1:T7:O02:D02, 8227:2020:M1:T7:O02:D03, 8227:2020:M1:T7:O03:D02, 8227:2020:M1:T7:O03:D03.

### Teaching across the books

YM begins with corner,centred and translated solids before vector notation,magnitude and operations. Goldie begins with2Ddirection/grid reading,then extends to3Dmagnitudes and cuboid/regular-prism routes. Both develop internal ratios,midpoints and collinearity proofs. Goldie’s formal vector-line sections exceed8227scope,but explicitly retained supplied-relation or Cartesian-accessible facets remain useful internal evidence.

### Examination comparison

Directly assessed NSSCAS parts: 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q6:a, 8227:EXAM:2022:P1:Q11:a, 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q7:a.i, 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q7:a.ii, 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q10:a, 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q10:b, 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q3:a.

Same-type textbook practice: 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:vector-endpoints-and-notation to 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q7:a.i.

Structural branches: 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:vector-spatial-coordinate-reading to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q10:a — The cuboid source reads vertex coordinates directly from three labelled edge lengths and an origin. The pyramid exam first locates its raised apex and the midpoint of BV and then subtracts A to obtain AN; the source is the easier coordinate-reading subtask, not coverage of that midpoint/displacement chain. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:vector-endpoints-and-notation to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q6:a — The selected source forms one displacement from two positions. The exam additionally multiplies AB by 3/2 and translates from A to an external point P; those signed section and translation decisions are absent from the source. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:vector-endpoints-and-notation to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q10:a — Both use an endpoint difference, but the source endpoints are supplied numerical coordinates. In the pyramid exam N must first be inferred as the midpoint of BV using the dimensions and apex height, before AN is formed. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:vector-endpoints-and-notation to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q10:b — The source explicitly supplies both endpoint positions, whereas the exam requires reading A and C from the oriented rectangular-base diagram before forming the diagonal AC. The subtraction response is shared; the source omits the spatial interpretation. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:vector-linear-combinations to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q6:a — The source adds two explicitly supplied free vectors componentwise. The exam additionally requires the signed external multiplier AP=(3/2)AB and translation from A. It is an easier arithmetic subtask; it does not practise the missing geometric decision. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:vector-linear-combinations to 8227:EXAM:2022:P1:Q11:a — The source adds two explicitly supplied free vectors componentwise. The exam additionally requires the direction and factor -2 in RQ parallel to OP, then OR=OQ-2OP. It is an easier arithmetic subtask; it does not practise the missing geometric decision. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:vector-linear-combinations to 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q7:a.i — The source adds two explicitly supplied free vectors componentwise. The exam additionally requires choosing the displacement order OB-OA. It is an easier arithmetic subtask; it does not practise the missing geometric decision. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:vector-linear-combinations to 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q7:a.ii — The source adds two explicitly supplied free vectors componentwise. The exam additionally requires using AB=BC to infer OC=2OB-OA. It is an easier arithmetic subtask; it does not practise the missing geometric decision. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:vector-linear-combinations to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q10:a — The source adds two explicitly supplied free vectors componentwise. The exam additionally requires constructing N from the midpoint of BV before subtracting A. It is an easier arithmetic subtask; it does not practise the missing geometric decision. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:vector-linear-combinations to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q10:b — The source adds two explicitly supplied free vectors componentwise. The exam additionally requires reading the endpoints and choosing the directed diagonal AC. It is an easier arithmetic subtask; it does not practise the missing geometric decision. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:vector-division-position to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q6:a — Both locate a point on the line through two supplied positions. The source uses an internal 2:3 division, with positive convex weights; the exam has AP=(3/2)AB, so P lies beyond B and the A weight is negative. The source omits that external-division sign decision. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:vector-division-position to 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q7:a.ii — The source forms a position from two endpoints and a given internal ratio. The exam instead supplies A and the midpoint B through AB=BC and recovers the missing endpoint C=2B-A. Reversing the section relation adds the unknown-endpoint decision. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:vector-geometric-basis-displacement to 8227:EXAM:2022:P1:Q11:a — Both choose directed paths from a geometric diagram and combine parallel sides. The hexagonal-prism source must infer regular-hexagon relations for several p,q,r expressions; the trapezium exam supplies the factor 2 and seeks one Cartesian position. The source has more edge-relation decisions, but no numeric coordinate substitution. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:vector-geometric-basis-displacement to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q10:a — Both infer a directed vector from a three-dimensional figure. The source uses abstract regular-hexagon edge relations p,q,r, while the exam uses orthogonal numeric base dimensions plus an apex-edge midpoint. Their common geometric path decision has different supporting geometry and response parameters. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:vector-geometric-basis-displacement to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q10:b — Both infer a directed vector from a three-dimensional figure. The source uses abstract regular-hexagon edge relations p,q,r, while the exam uses orthogonal numeric base dimensions to form a base diagonal. Their common geometric path decision has different supporting geometry and response parameters. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:vector-dot-product-direct to 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q3:a — The source computes a scalar product of fixed component vectors and stops at that scalar. The exam additionally requires forming the parameter-dependent vector and solving its zero-product equation. This is supporting arithmetic practice, not a complete exemplar of the exam response. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:vector-perpendicularity-parameter to 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q3:a — The selected source b-q finds one unknown component q from AB dot CD=0. The exam first forms pOA-OB and then determines its coefficient p by the zero dot product with OC. The source omits constructing that parameter-dependent linear combination. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:vector-midpoint-position to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q6:a — The selected source averages two supplied endpoint positions to locate their midpoint. The exam locates an external section point with multiplier 3/2, requiring signed unequal weights rather than the fixed internal 1/2 weights. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:vector-midpoint-position to 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q7:a.ii — The selected source finds the midpoint from both endpoints. The exam supplies A and midpoint B through AB=BC and asks for the missing endpoint C, so it must reverse the averaging relation rather than apply it forwards. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:vector-basis-division-displacement to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q6:a — Both choose a fractional displacement and add an origin path to locate a point. The source uses an internal E-to-F ratio 2:1 in an abstract triangle basis; the exam uses AP=(3/2)AB outside the segment and numerical Cartesian positions. External signed weights add a decision absent from the source. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:vector-basis-division-displacement to 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q7:a.i — Source (a) first takes DF-DE to obtain EF in an abstract basis and then scales it by 2/3 to form EG. The isolated exam part asks only for OB-OA. The source includes an extra given-ratio scaling response. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:vector-basis-division-displacement to 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q7:a.ii — The source builds an internally divided point from a given segment ratio and abstract edge basis. The exam instead reverses AB=BC to recover an endpoint from A and midpoint B, using numerical positions. The required direction of section recovery differs. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:vector-basis-division-displacement to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q10:a — Both locate a fractional point on an edge and obtain a directed vector by adding/subtracting positions. The source uses an abstract planar 2:1 triangle division; the exam first infers a three-dimensional apex and a BV midpoint from dimensions, then forms AN. The source omits those spatial-coordinate decisions. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:vector-spatial-edge-length-chain to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q10:a — Both interpret a raised roof/pyramid point from base dimensions and height and form a displacement. The source roof has a centred ridge of known length and asks for several edges; the exam uses an apex above the base centre and a midpoint of BV before AN. The spatial construction differs rather than only the numbers. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:vector-spatial-edge-length-chain to 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q10:b — The source (a)–(b) chain reads roof vertices and returns several spatial edges; the isolated exam part reads two rectangular-base vertices and returns their diagonal. The source adds the centred-ridge coordinate decisions. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:vector-spatial-edge-length-chain to 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q7:a.i — The source constructs roof coordinates and endpoint differences for four edges. The exam directly subtracts two supplied positions for AB, without interpreting a spatial model. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:vector-parallelogram-vertex-recovery to 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q6:a — Both construct a missing position vector by a directed vector path. YM uses equal opposite parallelogram sides OD=OA+OC-OB. 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q6:a instead uses the external-section relation AP=(3/2)AB to form OP=OA+(3/2)(OB-OA). The geometric relation/path changes, so this is a structural branch with no warranted scalar difficulty ordering; not a merely cosmetic context change. (mixed); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:vector-parallelogram-vertex-recovery to 8227:EXAM:2022:P1:Q11:a — Both recover a missing vertex by vector paths from parallel sides. YM uses a parallelogram’s equal sides, OD=OA+OC-OB; the exam must orient the trapezium’s parallel side and apply its length factor 2 before OR=OQ-2OP, then establish a supplied vector. The orientation/scale and show-that response add decisions. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:vector-parallelogram-vertex-recovery to 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q7:a.ii — Both construct a missing position vector by a directed vector path. YM uses equal opposite parallelogram sides OD=OA+OC-OB. 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q7:a.ii instead uses AB=BC to form OC=OB+AB=2OB-OA. The geometric relation/path changes, so this is a structural branch with no warranted scalar difficulty ordering; not a merely cosmetic context change. (mixed).

Demands without an NSSCAS part: none.

### Difficulty and grade descriptors

E07/A07name3Dcoordinates/notation;C07names addition,subtraction,scaling and geometric interpretation. Difficulty increases through frame choice,label incidence and proof connections,not simply a third nonzero component. A supplied affine identity is not permission to author arbitrary formal vector-line equations.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

YMTry1(b)copies the wrong prism’s coordinates;Try6Q2has an opposite-direction sign error. GoldieExample12(iii)omits the external ratio point. Equal vector directions alone do not prove point collinearity unless the vectors share the relevant point. These are registered source/author cautions,not claims of examiner-error frequency.

### Gaps and representations

Both books support routine component operations;Goldie offers a regular-prism symbolic basis,YM centred-coordinate practice. Generated diagrams will need consistent vertex frames and head/tail labels. Supporting motion/locus tasks with supplied relations do not establish additional prescribed formal-line coverage.

## Magnitude, unit vectors and sign of parallel direction

Demands: 8227:2020:M1:T7:O03:D01, 8227:2020:M1:T7:O04:D01.

### Teaching across the books

Both books compute norms before unit vectors and derived positions. YM4asks a unit vector PARALLELwhile later5specifies SAMEdirection;Goldie10B6/7explicitlynormalises given directions and10B12first derives an external point. Source examples displaying only a positive normalisation do not remove the negative answer when wording merely saysparallel.

The exam directly supplies OA and OB; the selected textbook initial-separation response first sets the supplied motion models to t=0 and forms the relative displacement (10,8), then takes its magnitude. This one added preparation step is the branch, not the boat context or 2D/3D coefficient change. Collision and speed responses are excluded. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:vector-side-length-perimeter to 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q7:b.i — The source forms all three triangle side displacements, evaluates their lengths and adds a perimeter. The exam stops at the magnitudes of two supplied position vectors. The source adds displacement construction, a third norm and the perimeter sum. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:vector-spatial-edge-length-chain to 8227:EXAM:2023:P1:Q7:b.i — The source must infer a roof displacement before finding its sloping length; the exam asks for magnitudes of two already supplied position vectors. The source adds coordinate modelling and displacement construction. (harder).

Demands without an NSSCAS part: 8227:2020:M1:T7:O03:D01.

### Difficulty and grade descriptors

E07/C07name unit,displacement and position vectors;A07names magnitude. Increased demand comes from deriving the target vector,selecting a direction sign or satisfying a requested nonunit magnitude. The zero vector must be invalid for normalisation even though its norm calculation is valid.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

YMExercise5Q1unit key has one wrong sign;Q6key supplies an unnormalised diagonal. Other suspected signs were cleared on native renders,includingExercise5Q5’snegative middle component. Independent checks should verify norm ANDparallel/direction,not merely compare printed components. No examiner-specific unit-vector error rate is claimed.

### Gaps and representations

Both books give extensive direct unit practice despite no sampled NSSCASdirect part. Parallel-only versus same-direction needs an answer-policy branch. A normalised joining vector and a raw joining vector are distinct outputs even when they share a source label.

## Scalar products, angle conventions and perpendicular parameters

Demands: 8227:2020:M1:T7:O04:D02, 8227:2020:M1:T7:O05:D01, 8227:2020:M1:T7:O05:D02.

### Teaching across the books

YM pairs magnitude-angle and component definitions of the scalar product,then contrasts angle,parallel and perpendicular conditions. Goldie asks explicit proof/assumption prompts and develops obtuse/opposite vectors before spatial angle problems. Both later solve parameterised angle and length conditions. The common-vertex choice and angle convention are retained,not hidden inside an acosformula.

Demands without an NSSCAS part: none.

### Difficulty and grade descriptors

C07/A07include scalar-product angles and perpendicularity. Extra difficulty is choosing the two correct arms,constructing a spatial model,or checking unsquared sign after a parameter equation. Opposite vectors give180degrees;an acute line angle instead uses the specifically requested convention. Grade descriptions do not justify replacing every angle by its acute supplement.

### Pitfalls and evidential limits

Goldie10F10requires rejecting a squared-equation root with the wrong cosine sign;YM16andTry18Q1preserve valid correlated signs. A perpendicularity equation may have no real parameter when the dot is25+p². ERR09corroborates the orthogonality principle;the exact squared-angle traps remain textbook/author cautions rather than reported learner frequencies.

### Gaps and representations

Both books offer direct given-vector angles and richer diagrams. Roof/cube/pyramid reconstruction adds geometry before the same final dot calculation;these must not collapse into a routine component pair. A polar direction measured from+xdoesnot assess the same response as a three-point angle.
